    - His first thought for it, the card's layout etched as a skeleton loader shows it, and a mirror finish over the whole face. D is satin rays inside a mirror edge. So "no band, no window" in "A sleeping card is a blank" stands again as written: a blank shows nothing of the layout.
    - A · Etched layout, B · The mould and C · Facets, which are passed over.
- Taken with the pick, not said in so many words, and his to overturn: the long lights off the screen, which he asked about and which light D in the study, and D's own change, the band of light.

**What he likes in D, and what must not be lost (Nov 13)**

Fable had told the builder to fix two things in D: a boss that "can read as a ring" and a dark fan that "can read as a smudge". He stopped the prompt before sending it. Both are what he likes.

- **The handle.** "Is that the handle you're referring to? Currently I actually enjoy how it looks 3D. I'm not opposed to making it better, but I don't necessarily want to lose the essence of it. What it looks like to me is like a handle on a swinging door on an antique china cabinet or something, some decadent handle on a piece of furniture. I really like the vibe of that."
    - In exact terms: the raised round piece at the middle of the dial stays, and it is called the handle. It reads as a turned handle on a fine piece of furniture, with real depth. It may be made richer. It is never made flatter, plainer or smaller, and never taken away.
- **The shadow.** "The smudge you're referring to, it actually looks like a shadow. The background interacts with the light much higher quality than the other three cards. That's why I chose it." "I want to keep the dark area that's obviously a shadow and improve upon it further if that's even possible. I don't want to make it worse, or possibly remove it."
    - In exact terms: the dark area that runs out from the handle and swings as the light moves stays, and it reads as a shadow. It may be deepened. It is never removed, weakened or tidied away.
    - What it is in the study, so that it is kept on purpose: not a cast shadow. The rays smear the room's lights round the dial, except where they run along the lights' bands, and there the dark of the room shows through. So it pivots on the handle as the scroll and the cursor's light move.
- **Where it can go further.** "I'm definitely open to improving it even further, by making it feel like the user is looking at something in real 3D space, because of how the interactions of the light reflect on it are hyperrealistic."
    - In exact terms: the light on the dial is why he picked it. More of that is welcome: the card reading as a real object in real space. Nothing that makes the light on it less real.

**His look at the handle as Claude Code first built it (Nov 14)**

Said to the web chat with a screenshot from his phone, while Claude Code was still working on the fourth pass. Nothing had been pushed, so the web chat has seen the screenshot and not the code.

- "He changed the center. It no longer looks like a knob for a piece of furniture. He changed it to look more like a real dial. I don't like that it's off center and not protruding more from the background. Before it felt like an object poking out of the card, which is what made it so good. Now it looks like a slight raise."
- In exact terms, three things are wrong with the handle as built:
    1. It is off center. It belongs at the dead center of the card.
    2. It barely stands off the card. In the study it read as an object poking out of the card. It has to stand at least that far off, and plainly.
    3. It reads as part of a watch dial: flat rings round a small raised middle. It has to read as a knob from a piece of antique furniture.
- What he thought he was getting, from two photographs he attached of antique cabinet knobs in aged bronze: a round head with a ring of beads at its rim and a flower in relief on its face, one with swirling petals and one a sunflower with a beaded middle, on a short stem with a flared foot. They are a shop's product photographs, so they are not in this repo, which is public.
- In exact terms: the handle is a knob of that family, designed for this site and not copied from either photograph.
- Everything else about D stands: the rays, the shadow that swings off the handle, the band of light.
- Fable's part in it, said to him: the prompt told the builder to judge the blank against a photograph of a real engine turned dial. That may be what pulled the middle toward a watch part. Not confirmed, since the builder's report has not been seen.

**The knob, built (Nov 14). He has looked: "This looks great! Great work on the card."**

- At `/lab/work`, and alone at `/lab/work-blank`. Built in answer to "His look at the handle as Claude Code first built it".
- His look (Nov 14), said to Claude Code after opening both pages: "This looks great! Great work on the card." Read as: the knob on the blank is what he asked for. He went straight on to two faults in the band of light, which are in "His look at the fourth pass", below. He did not go through the calls listed here one by one, so each is still his to overturn.
- What it is: the head of an antique cabinet knob, seen from straight in front, at the dead center of the card. A rolled rim, a ring of 30 beads, a half round moulding, and a domed face with a rosette in relief: eight broad petals with a crease down each, eight long pointed leaves behind them, a plain boss at the heart. In old bronze: dark where it is low, rubbed bright where it stands proud.
- How it stands off the card, seen head on:
  - Its own shadow on the dial. The head stands on a stem, so its shadow is the head's round shape lying clear of the head, away from the strongest light, and swings as the cursor's light and the stream move. With nothing near it lies up and to the right, along the dark wedge he picked the study for, so the wedge reads as the long end of the knob's own shadow.
  - The light across its dome and its beads: the side facing a light is lit and the far side is dark, and that follows the cursor's light too.
  - The head moves against the dial as the card turns under the pointer.
- **Calls made for him. None is a rule:**
  - The rosette's design, which is this site's own: eight petals over eight leaves, turned a little as it goes out. It copies neither photograph.
  - The head is 92 of the card's 460 units across. The study's handle was about 60.
  - The first build leaned the handle's dome toward the eye by where the card stood on the screen, which is what put it off center. That is gone. The head is drawn at the card's center and moves only when the card itself turns.
  - The turned rings round the first handle are gone. The rays run under the knob to its stem.
  - Its bronze is a little browner than the dial's, so it reads as a separate, older thing.
  - The knob draws back into the dial as the band of light comes to it, and is gone before the band is on it. It stands out again when the band has run back past it. A band that crossed it standing cut it in two.
  - With nothing near, the knob is lit from down and to the left, the side the dial's bright fan lies on. Lit from the other side its shadow fell opposite the wedge, and the two read as shadows of two lights.

**The fourth pass on the Work page: the agreement for the build (Nov 13)**

One round in Claude Code, on `/lab/work`. It takes in everything he has ruled since the third pass. He held every prompt until all of it was answered: "I only want to go to Claude Code once we have everything figured out that's open."

**What gets built**

1. **The Sun stones' strike:** a little under half of what it throws now, scattered, nothing aimed ("His look at the third pass, after the blank", 1).
2. **The left side of the path is the right side, flipped** (the same, 2).
3. **The bolt runs only from the landing on the category bar to the meeting under the button.** Above and below, the path as it was before the bolt (the same, 3). The switch between the two paths, and `?path=now`, come off the page.
4. **A strike does not color a card's picture** (the same, 4).
5. **A card is asleep whenever the stream is not on it.** It wakes at the strike and goes back to sleep when the stream draws back above it ("A card sleeps again").
    - **Switched off on the home page for now, by his word (Nov 20): Open 37.** Items 5 to 8 are set aside while the switch is off. The blank and its band are kept.
6. **The blank is D · Engine turned,** made in the card's own plate and lit by long lights that stand off the screen ("His pick of a blank").
7. **The change between asleep and awake is D's:** a band of light that crosses the card with the card behind it, and runs back to put it to sleep.
8. **On a press, a row the stream has not reached does nothing** ("His reaction to the ideas").
9. **"Case study" gets a lighter resting color,** so it passes contrast with the cursor's light behind it.

**The blank is a goal. The study is the floor to beat, not a drawing to copy**

- What it has to be: an engine turned plate as a watchmaker turns a dial, in the site's bronze. Fine rays round the card's middle, the handle at the dead center, the card's polished edge round it. The handle is a knob from antique furniture, not a part of a watch ("His look at the handle as Claude Code first built it", above). The lights off the screen smear across the rays into fans, and the fans turn as the page scrolls, as the cursor's light moves beside the card, and as the stream comes near.
- Kept from the study, and only ever made better ("What he likes in D", above): the handle, with its depth, and the dark area that reads as a shadow and swings on the handle.
- Where the builder can go further: the whole card reading as a real object in real space, by how the light behaves on it.
- Fixed: nothing of the card's layout or content. The card's size and its edge. Rays about the middle, with the handle. The shadow.
- Free: how fine the rays are, a groove inside the edge, where the lights stand and how bright they are, and anything that makes the handle richer or the light more real.
- It is built in the card's plate (`src/metal/`), where it takes the cursor's light and the stream's. The study's canvas laid over the cards is not the way.

**The change is a goal too**

- The band starts where the stream strikes the card and crosses away from it. Going back to sleep it runs back the way it came.
- It is light with weight, lit by the strike: the dial on one side of it and the card on the other. Never a plain fade, and never a soft wipe.
- Under the pointer or with keyboard focus a sleeping card wakes by the same change, and goes back to sleep by it when the pointer leaves.

**What does not get built**

- A phone or any screen under 1100 px.
- The blanks he passed over. A sun, a line or a hallmark on the blank.
- Anything else on the site sleeping until the stream arrives. That theme is still open (Open 21).
- A change to the surge's front, the fuse under the button or the burst at the leading end. "The surge looks much better."
- The top bar. Real projects for the other three categories. New pictures. The lunge.

**What stays exactly as approved**

- `/lab/stream` and the approved buttons, pixel for pixel.
- The card awake: the data plate, `CARD_LOOK`, one size, one row of tags.
- The stream's look, its shards and sparks, riding shards staying with it, no second wave.
- The stones' faces lit. The surge at 1.1 seconds, both sides together, with the core turned down ahead of the front.
- The rail's brackets and the finial as they are, and the line in the card's stylesheet that keeps a foot on a card with no links.

**Fable's calls in this agreement. He has not ruled on them and can overturn any**

- The band starts at the strike. In the study it starts at the top of the card, since the study has no stream. The idea as first put to him had it crossing "from the strike".
- A sleeping card under the pointer wakes by the same change and goes back to sleep by it.
- The blank and its change are given to the builder as goals, as the bolt was.
- A blank is never brighter than the stream beside it. The lights off the screen are set to suit the page, not copied from the study, which has no stream.
- Carried from before: what lands on a sleeping card does not wake it, and with reduced motion or under 1100 px no card sleeps.

**The fourth pass, built (Nov 14). He has looked at the blank and the band. The rest waits on him**

- At `/lab/work`, by Claude Code on his Mac. All nine are on the page. The blank can be seen alone at `/lab/work-blank`, Study 20, with a button on each card that wakes it and puts it back to sleep.
- His look is in "His look at the fourth pass", below: the card with the knob is great, and the band had two faults, since mended. Nothing else here is settled until he says so.
- The test shot at `/lab/stream` and the approved buttons were compared with stills from before: every still agrees pixel for pixel. The awake card's stills agree except for the words "Case study", whose color was changed on purpose.
- Fable's calls in the agreement were built as written: the band starts at the strike, a sleeping card under the pointer or with keyboard focus wakes and sleeps by the same change, no card sleeps with reduced motion or under 1100 px, and what lands on a sleeping card does not wake it. The lights were set for the page.
- **Where the build had to choose between two things he asked for.** His to overturn:
  - "The left side must be the exact mirror of the right" and "above the bar, put the path back the way it was". Before the bolt the two sides were not quite a mirror above the bar: each swayed in its own way and leaned a little differently, so that they never landed in the same instant. The build mirrors everywhere the sides are apart. So above the bar the left side is now the right side's old path, turned over, and not its own.
  - The word WORK's ink is 2 px to the right of the middle of the page, where the bar, the cards, the stones and the button stand. The left side is the right's mirror about the middle of the word while it is on the word, and about the middle of the button from the bar down, and moves from one to the other on its way down to the bar.
- **Calls made while building.** None is a rule. All are in `LOG.md`, "The fourth pass on the Work page, built". The ones he is most likely to have a view on:
  - The band of light is drawn by the card's own plate. It lies under the card's words, which are uncovered at its line, and not over them.
  - The pointer's band sets out from where the pointer came onto the card. With keyboard focus it sets out from the card's top.
  - The band takes about half a second when the stream wakes a card or leaves it, and about a third under the pointer.
  - The handle is a knob from antique furniture ("The knob, built", above). As first built it was a polished dome in turned rings, which he rejected.
  - The lights are the study's own, put at real places off the screen and run off into the room, so the shadow's line turns as the page scrolls.
  - There are two darks on the handle. The first is the one he picked: the room showing along the rays that run across the lights, a narrow wedge that slides along its line as the page scrolls. The second is new: the knob's own shadow on the dial, the round shape of its head lying away from whichever light is strongest on it. With nothing near it lies along the wedge. With the cursor's light near it lies away from that, and as the stream's leading end passes, away from that. It is what answers the cursor, and what says how far the knob stands off the card.
  - The stream's leading end lights a blank as one warm light as it comes near, which lies on the rays as a soft fan.
  - The band is a bowed front that spreads from the struck point. It is broad and hot where it starts and thins to a line by the far side, and it lights the dial's rays ahead of it.
  - While the pointer's band is crossing a card, the card behind it is whole and in its colors. They are not faded in as well.
  - A stone throws 45 percent of what it threw. The budget for thrown shards stays at 400: three quick presses still reach it.
  - "Case study" at rest is three parts the quietest text color to two parts the next one up.

**His look at the fourth pass (Nov 14): two faults in the band, and one rule**

Said to Claude Code after opening `/lab/work` and `/lab/work-blank`.

- "This looks great! Great work on the card. There are 2 other things we need to fix though."
- "If I scroll down and change the category a few times and then scroll up to where the 1st row is the placeholder cards, and then switch categories, you can see the actual cards come in even though the surge is not touching it for a split second before the animation to turn it to the placeholder plays."
- "If I scroll at normal speed I see multiple overlaps. I don't know how to explain it, but when the surge touches the side of the card, that's where the card comes into life at the starting point. I see multiple of those starting points overlapping if I scroll slightly faster."
- "On the second link that you gave me, pressing the wake button on all the cards, all of them have issues. Some of them have text that is barely visible still on the gold part while it's coming in. This shouldn't happen. Other ones have darkness that comes in on one side of the card when it's coming in."
- "I just think we need to revisit that animation and make sure it's consistent and doesn't show text is not supposed to yet until the animation passes it, and we need to make it so it only ever opens from the first spot the surge touches and can't open from multiple spots."
- "The surge" here is the stream's leading end as he scrolls, and "the placeholder" and "the gold part" are the blank.
- In exact terms, four rules for the change between asleep and awake:
    1. **A card opens from one place only: the first spot the stream touches.** There is never a second band on a card, and a card never opens from two places.
    2. **None of a card's words shows on the blank.** A word is seen only once the band has passed it.
    3. **Nothing dark comes in at a side or a corner as the band crosses.**
    4. **A press on a sleeping row never shows a card,** not for one frame. This is item 8 of the agreement, which the build did not hold to.
- He has not ruled on the rest of the fourth pass: the stones' strike, the mirror, the bolt's ends, "Case study".

**The band, mended (Nov 14), waiting on his look**

- At `/lab/work` and `/lab/work-blank`. All four were found in real frames recorded from a live page, and none could be seen in a filmstrip of frozen frames, which is how the band had been judged.
- What each was:
    1. Two starting points. A card had two bands, one for the stream and one for the pointer. His pointer rests on the page while he scrolls, so each card came under it and opened from where the pointer met it, and then the stream struck and opened it again from the strike.
    2. Words on the blank. The card's plate was drawn before the stream had set that frame's band, and the mask on the words after. So the words were uncovered one frame ahead of the band, up to about 30 px at the band's fastest.
    3. The dark side. One number in the band's light ran past what a number can hold far ahead of the band, and the card's far corner was drawn black for the first frame or two.
    4. The card that flashed. A card that is put away kept what it was when it went. Shown again in a sleeping place, it was drawn awake for one frame. And one that had been under the pointer when it went came back awake and ran a band back to sleep.
- What is built:
    - A card has one band. It belongs to the place the card stands in, and every card of that place wears it, the ones put away too.
    - Where a band sets out from is chosen only while the card is wholly asleep or wholly awake. Whatever comes or goes while it is crossing turns that one band round.
    - The plates are drawn last in a frame, after the band is set, and the words come up a little behind the band's line, in its trailing light.
    - A card that is put away is no longer under the pointer, and is drawn as its place is before it is seen again.
- **Calls made for him. None is a rule:**
    - **A pointer at rest that the page is scrolled under does not wake a sleeping card.** This narrows a line of the agreement, "Under the pointer or with keyboard focus a sleeping card wakes by the same change". A pointer that is put on a sleeping card, and keyboard focus, still wake it, by one band from where the pointer came on. Without this, a card under his resting pointer would open from the pointer and not from the stream, which is what he ruled out.
    - On a touch screen larger than a phone the stream wakes a card, and so does a tap on it (Cards and buttons, "No card lights by itself"). Before, the cards nearest the middle of the screen woke by a band of their own.
    - A card the pointer has already woken does not open again when the stream reaches it. The strike still flares its rim and throws its sparks.
    - A blank does not grow under a pointer at rest. It grows as it wakes.
    - Where a card goes back to sleep to: where the stream struck it if the stream was the last thing keeping it awake, and where the pointer came on if the pointer was.

**How it is built**

He added these while the stream was a simulated liquid. They hold for anything in the stream that is simulated: shards thrown loose, sparks.

- The simulation is stepped from the one clock (`src/world/clock.ts`) with a fixed timestep and a seed, so any frame can be frozen and shot again exactly. Each new pour takes a new seed, so no two splashes match. (Nov 11)
- A particle budget is set for his Mac and for a phone before building, and what the phone gives up is said out loud. The phone view is checked as the work goes, not at the end. (Nov 11) **Changed Nov 13:** how a phone looks and where the stream runs on it now wait for the mobile pass (How we work, "The mobile pass"). The budget for a phone is still set out loud.

**How it got here**

Kept so nobody walks the same road twice. Nothing in this list is a rule now.

- A stream of molten metal runs down the whole site. "I agree molten metal is the way to go." (Nov 11)
- It is called the pour. The name is Fable's and he has not objected. It is never called water, in anything written for a model or shown to a visitor. His reason: "if I call it water, then the model is gonna build it as if it acts like water". (Nov 11)
- It is the site's own copper, melted: it glows in the palette's light colors, ember to flame to hot to pale. It looks like a foundry pour: bright, clean and quick, throwing sparks and beads. It never looks like lava (dark, crusted, slow), fire (flames) or water (thin, clear). (Fable's recommendation, which he agreed to, Nov 11)
- The first thing built is one test shot, alone on a blank page: it falls, hits a ledge, splashes, runs along the ledge and drips off the end. Nothing is built around the pour until he says that shot looks incredible. `QUALITY.md` says what the shot has to show. (Fable's proposal, Nov 11. He went ahead on it without objecting.)
- How the test shot is built, which he said "Go" to (Nov 11): a real particle fluid simulated side on in the flat plane of the page, with gravity, cohesion and adhesion. Only the route is planned. The particles are merged into one rounded body and lit per pixel. Each particle carries a temperature that sets its color on the palette. Its glow lights the ledge and the page, and the ledge keeps a fading heat mark. The test page has two controls: pour again, and slow motion.
- His verdict on the first test shot (Nov 11). The material is still liquid metal: "the idea here that we're going with is liquid metal". What he saw was not it:
  - "I definitely don't like how it puddles."
  - "The mainstream is blasted back multiple times due to this puddling", and from "the way the particles can bounce backwards and hit the stream, pushing it back".
  - "Instead of looking like a refined beautiful hot liquid metal, this looks more like mud splashing around and puddling up."
- In exact terms: the metal must not heap up where it lands, nothing it throws may knock the falling stream off its line, and the whole of it has to read as refined, not as a mess. (Fable's restatement, Nov 11)
- He wants something special about the stream itself. "Think like how plasma looks that liquid metal color, but with shiny stuff all in it, or maybe something else. I'm open to options." (Nov 11)
- His verdict on the three looks at `/lab/pour-looks` (Nov 11): "I think the best looking one is A, but if being honest it's still about a 5.5/10." And: "Maybe the Liquid Metal isn't the right direction."
- He asked for nothing more to be built until he had talked it through. He then asked for sketches twice (the next lines). (Nov 11)
- Three rough sketches of abstract ideas are at `/lab/stream-ideas`: Threads, Copper leaf and Ink. He asked for them rough: "they don't have to be super high res. I just need to get a general idea. Once I choose a general idea, we can work really hard in building that one out really well." (Nov 11)
- He picked Copper leaf: "I already know I wanna go with the middle option." (Nov 11)
- He asked to see a core of light with shards two ways: "I'd like to see your recommendation. and then I'd like to see the same thing, but instead of the smooth light, can we go with the arc? I like to compare the two." And: "let's keep it simple for now, and the next iteration will go ultra high quality." Both are at `/lab/stream-core`. (Nov 11)
- Before the finished version is built: "I think we need a plan and a concise agreement before we do the handoff to Claude to build it." (Nov 11)

### Hero

- The floating future city: a made up city, a spire at the center, parks, a Star Trek feel. The model from the second build is kept.
- The city sits along the bottom of the screen with the text above it. The model never goes behind the text.
- The opening uses the whole screen with nothing cut off at the top or bottom. The city builds itself in the middle, then moves down as the text comes in.
- At rest the text sits about a third of the way down. On a desktop it is fine if half the city or a little more shows.
- He wants camera movement and a real moment of awe.
- No PC parts in the hero.

- The city as built is not what the site is built on. "It's not like it's a masterpiece that we're building the whole site off of. That's the stream." It "is going to need a massive overhaul and the current version will probably just be shown as a draft anyways". (Nov 12)
- The opening animation as carried from the second build is "five out of 10 at best" and "definitely needs to be redone". The city itself "looks good" and "could still use some work". The city gets revisited anyway so it ties in with the stream. (Nov 12)
- **The hero is next in the web chat, layout first (Nov 14).** "Now I was hoping we could take a look at the landing page. I'm mainly concerned with the layout now, something we've never really dialed in. City will need to be completely redone, with a brand new opening animation. At the end of the opening animation, the city goes down to the bottom of your screen on the desktop viewport. Some of the city is obscured, including the waterfalls, and the main one that the surge will be on. Scrolling down reveals them, continue scrolling down and you'll notice the surge gets caught and starts going as you scroll." "As far as layout, what are some good options? I'm also interested in the content you proposed to put there. I have some text for it now that may be helpful. Writing our opening paragraph."
    - In exact terms:
    - The city is made again, and so is the opening. Both were already ruled as needing it (Nov 12, above).
    - When the opening ends the city rests along the bottom of a desktop screen with its lower part below the screen's foot. What is hidden there includes the city's falls, and the main fall, which is where the stream begins.
    - Scrolling brings the hidden part up into view. Scrolling further, the stream's leading end leaves the main fall and runs on down the page with the scroll, as it does everywhere else.
    - "Waterfalls" and "surge" are his words for the city's falls and the stream. The falls were ruled Nov 11 to be the stream's own light and not what the second build drew.
    - Open: the layout of the words and the city, and what the words block holds. He has text for the opening paragraph and has not sent it yet. Fable's options are put to him in the chat, in words. Nothing is drawn or built.
- **His rough draft of the hero's words (Nov 14).** "I'm definitely open to change it, as it's just a rough draft." A line above the name: "Product Engineer · Designer". The name: "Brandon Church". First paragraph: "I take products from the first client conversation all the way to production. I'm the person clients trust to own the outcome, not just hand over the artifacts." Second paragraph: "We're heading into the most exciting stretch of technology in human history. Discovery is speeding up, and the tools we build now will shape how people live for generations. I want my work to be part of that, in software and in the physical world."
    - Not ruled. Fable's view, given in the chat: the line, the name and the first paragraph are the hero. The second paragraph is about 45 words on top of 30, which is too many for a screen the city has to share, and it reads better in About. The words are his.
- **Layout A, the first paragraph only, and go (Nov 14).** "I'll go with your recommendation. You're free to go ahead and build out the layout."
    - In exact terms: the hero's words are centered about a third of the way down, over the city, which lies full width along the bottom with its spire on the center line and its main fall under the spire. The block holds the line above his name, his name, his first paragraph and two buttons, one to Work and one to Contact. His second paragraph leaves the hero for About.
    - What is drawn next is a wireframe of that, in the web chat, with the city resting low, its falls below the foot of the screen, and the stream setting off from the main fall on scroll. Outlines only: the city's look and the opening's motion are not in it.
    - The first paragraph is drawn in his own words as he sent them. The shorter version Fable offered is his to take or leave.
- **His look at the wireframe: yes to the layout (Nov 14).** "I like it, a lot! Obviously we're going to need to revisit the city to figure out exactly how it's going to look now that we've completely changed the art style. Overall, though, the opening animation is good. I like that it happens in the center of the screen and there's no like crazy camera movements. The outline, though, I don't know if that's actually gonna work for like a hyper realistic 3D model, and when I say hyper realistic, I mean something that looks like it's real, not necessarily something that's realistic in the real world. The city should be a futuristic sci-fi looking city, and it should have a beautiful glow around it, especially going upwards. I'm actually wondering if we should make like a night sky as well. Something that doesn't get in the way of the content of the hero, but up top and in the corners and on the sides, maybe like some stars or something."
    - In exact terms:
    - **Layout A stands** as drawn at `/lab/hero-draft`: the words, how much of the city shows, the falls under the foot of the screen, the stream setting off on scroll.
    - **The opening's order stands:** the city comes to be whole in the middle of the screen, then moves down to where it rests, then the words come in. With no large camera moves. This changes "He wants camera movement" above: the awe is to come from the city, not from the camera.
    - **The city drawing itself as an outline is not settled.** That was the wireframe's stand in. He doubts a line drawing suits a model that looks real. How the real city comes to be is open.
    - **The city's look:** a futuristic science fiction city that looks like a real thing, though not a thing of this world, with a glow round it that is strongest going up. It is made again to sit with the site as it now looks.
    - **A night sky is his question, not a ruling:** stars at the top, in the corners and at the sides of the hero, never behind or among the words.
    - Fable's answers are given in the chat and are not rules.
- **What he meant by the glow and the outline, and the opening waits for the city (Nov 14).** "When I said a glow going up, what I actually meant was like my portrait image, how I have that glow around me. I just want the city to feel alive, like when you see a city off in the distance, you can see the glow going into the sky." "I don't want the opening animation to be like an animation that outlines the city." "I just saw the opening animation, how like it goes up to the right and then down and outlines the buildings. That's what I meant. I just don't want animation that does that." "I'm not 100% sure how I want the opening animation to look. It's kind of hard to envision it without already seeing the city in place. It might make sense to put a pause on that until Claude Code actually builds the city itself, and then once we have the asset, we can build that animation."
    - In exact terms:
    - **The glow** is a soft glow behind and above the city, of the kind behind his portrait in About: the light a far off city throws into the sky. It is not a light that climbs the buildings and not part of the opening.
    - **No opening that traces the city's outline.** The wireframe's lines running up and down each building are the thing he does not want.
    - **How the opening looks is paused** until the city is built. Fable agrees. What stays settled about it: it happens in the middle of the screen, the city ends resting low with the words above it, the city is never behind the words, and there are no large camera moves.
    - **The order of work this sets:** the city is made first, as a finished thing at rest. The opening is designed on the real city afterwards.
    - The three ways for the city to come to be that Fable put to him (built from shards, lit, risen) are not picked and wait with the opening.
    - The night sky is still his question, unanswered.
- **The opening is paused, and the night sky is drawn to be judged (Nov 14).** "Fully in agreement with everything you said here. Let's go ahead and build out the night sky and get an idea of what that looks like, and if it looks good, we can pass this update to Claude Code."
    - In exact terms: the opening's look waits until the city is built. The city is made first, at rest, with its glow. The night sky is drawn into the wireframe at `/lab/hero-draft` with Fable's three limits (the top, the corners and the sides only, pale and warm, sparse, and fading near the city's glow), for him to say yes or no to.
    - He wants the city's work added to what Claude Code already has in hand, once he has seen the sky.
- **Yes to the night sky, with more in it (Nov 14).** "Looks great, honestly. The only thing I would kind of want is a little bit more detail on the stars. I feel like right now they're all kind of just [dots]. It'd be nice if some of them actually look like stars, you know, with the 4 points? I'm also open to putting other stuff in the night sky too. Not sure what would be appropriate, but I'd want everything there to look hyper realistic and ultra detailed like you're looking at the night sky of another planet."
    - In exact terms: the sky stays, with its limits. Some of its stars, the brightest few, are drawn as stars with four points of light and not as dots. Other things may be in it. Whatever is there looks real and finely detailed: the night sky of another world.
    - What else goes in it is not ruled. Fable's proposal, drawn roughly in the wireframe and written into the agreement for the builder to show him: a moon of that world in an upper corner, a faint band of far stars across the other upper corner, and now and then a meteor. All in the palette, none behind the words, none brighter than the city.
- **His look at the city while Claude Code is building it (Nov 14).** "First off, the spire isn't how I'd like. In the original 3D city model that we built, which should be in the repo, it has rings around the tower. I like that look much more. We're also not using as much of the metal accent colors as I'd like on the city to tie it all together. Next, the whole city is, well, just a city. It doesn't have other aspects to it besides buildings. The original had vehicles flying around, nature areas, and so on. I don't necessarily wanna specify exactly what I want, but I wanna give him the freedom to utilize all these things to make something that's not so monotonous. Think of a future city from Star Trek or something, with ancient Egypt flair, gold, and bronze, and shininess, and so on."
    - In exact terms, from the still he sent:
    - **The spire.** Now: a plain tapering tower with a needle. To be: a spire with rings round it, as the carried city has (`src/pieces/city/`, `reference/pieces/city.jpg`). The carried city is the reference for that one thing and for what a city holds. It is still not the model that is used.
    - **The metal.** Now: the city is nearly all grey, with thin lines of orange light. To be: more of the site's own metals on it, gold, bronze and copper, polished and catching light, so it belongs with the cards, the stones and the buttons.
    - **More than buildings.** Now: towers on a disc and nothing else. To be: the other things a living city has. The carried one had flying vehicles and green places. What and how much is the builder's: he gives that freedom on purpose.
    - **The feel:** a city of the future as Star Trek draws one, with the flair of ancient Egypt.
    - **Corrected the same evening.** "We didn't have green places. Our nature areas still was in the color scheme of the site." "What I meant by it doesn't have to have those things, I just don't want the whole thing to be like a giant city with nothing else in it. It should feel like a whole world. Imagine a floating city in the sky, it would have some nature there and farms or whatever else. I just don't want the whole thing to look monotonous, like New York City, just endless buildings. It needs some diversity."
        - In exact terms: the city is a small world on its floating ground, not a block of towers from rim to rim. Towers at the middle, and round them other kinds of place: gardens, terraces, farms, open ground, whatever the builder chooses. Nature is in the site's own colors, warm and golden, not green. No one of these is required. Sameness is what is ruled out.
        - What the carried city has, seen in `reference/pieces/city.jpg`: a spire with a ringed deck and a sphere near its top, a few towers of different shapes close round it, a domed building, a broad avenue to the rim, stepped garden terraces round the edge, small craft in the air, and open ground between all of them. Most of its disc is not towers.
    - **This widens "The look" for the city.** `reference/kits/README.md` says only copper is orange and it is an accent, with everything else near neutral. For the city he wants gold and bronze used freely. The rule's reason still holds: the city must not become one orange mass, and its materials must still be told apart.
- **His idea: not a platform, a torn piece of a small planet with its core showing (Nov 14). Being checked with him before it goes to Claude Code.** "Instead of it being like a floating city in the sky, that's on like a platform, I kind of had this vision that this is like a little world, right? But obviously it's art, so things are really big in comparison to what the size of the planet would be. But we still have a lot of area to make something really big and diverse and grand. This should be the best 3D object on the whole site, so the bar is very high. I'm thinking like, not a half circle, but maybe a little bit less. That kind of look like a planet, and instead of it being perfectly cut off like another platform, let's make it look like this planet was ripped in half, and there's like the streams coming out in various points, and then you can actually see an animated 3D core of the planet, kind of just like flaming there, and coming out of it is our particle stream."
    - Fable's reading, put to him to confirm: the ground is the top of a small planet, a dome a little less than half a sphere, with the world on its curved top and things on it far larger than a planet's would be. Its underside is not cut clean. It is torn, as if the planet had been ripped in half. Streams of the stream's light come out of the torn face at several places. In the torn face the planet's core can be seen, a 3D thing that burns and moves, and the site's stream comes out of the core. It is to be the best 3D thing on the site.
    - **Confirmed, with debris and more room before Work (Nov 14).** "You read the curve side up, correct." "Yes, it seems like you have a great picture." "I want the user to look like they're looking at like a real thing, so it has to look like a hyper realistic object, even though that may not be a realistic scene you would see in the universe." "One more thing I want to add around the bottom part that's been ripped up, I would love to have like debris right there, like orbiting around and like tidally locked and you know all these different variations to make like the planet really did just like get destroyed halfway. This also goes well with the particles in the stream. And we can add more space between the hero section and the work section to let that really be appreciated."
        - In exact terms: the reading above is right, curved side up. It has to look like a real object that was photographed, though nothing like it exists.
        - **Debris** round the torn underside: pieces of the planet, some in slow orbit round it, some holding their place with one face to the core, some tumbling, in many sizes and kinds, so it reads as a world that really was broken in half. It is kin to the shards in the stream.
        - **More room between the hero and Work,** so the torn underside, the core and the debris can be looked at by themselves before Work comes up.
        - The agreement below is changed to this, and the wireframe at `/lab/hero-draft` is redrawn for it.
    - **What this changes in the rules above, each said to him in the chat:** "On a desktop it is fine if half the city or a little more shows" becomes: at rest the spire and the crown of the world show, about a third of the whole thing, and the rest comes up on the scroll. "The opening uses the whole screen with nothing cut off at the top or bottom" cannot hold at the page's size for a world taller than a screen: that is for the opening, when it is designed.
- **His look at the torn world as first built (Nov 14, near midnight).** From two stills of Claude Code's build, kept as `reference/studies/58-hero-the-world-as-first-built-whole.jpg` and `-at-rest.jpg`. "I think the planet itself looks awesome, but the city is a bit off. For one, the mountain does not read as a mountain. The flying cars look bad. The nature area does look good. I think we could use one more natural area on the left side. In general, maybe the city is too big? I like how dense it is but maybe it covers slightly too much going to the edge? What do you think, let's plan the final polished version." "I also think it's missing that shiny metal look. I don't see any of it. We could do some really cool things with it. Remember this is art so we have some flexibility."
    - In exact terms, from the stills:
    - **The planet stands as built:** the torn underside, the core, the debris. He likes it.
    - **The mountain.** Now: at the back right, a tall flat face of mottled grey and gold with a building standing on its top. It has no peak, no ridges and no slopes. To be: a thing anyone calls a mountain.
    - **The craft in the air.** Now: rows of small lozenges with no form. To be: good, or gone.
    - **The nature area** at the left, by the pyramid, in copper and bronze: good, kept.
    - **One more natural area.** He said "on the left side". The one there now is on the left. Which side he means is being confirmed with him.
    - **The city's reach.** Now: buildings cover the surface to its edge all the way round. His question, not a ruling: it may reach a little too far. He likes how dense it is.
    - **Polished metal.** Now: the buildings are matte grey with flat orange trims, and nothing on the world reflects. To be: metal that shines, used boldly. It is art, and he gives freedom for that.
    - **The plan agreed, with three changes of his (Nov 14, midnight).** "I'm thinking a nature area on the opposite side, sorry for the confusion, but I wanted it to look completely different. I'm actually pretty underwhelmed by the current nature area. It's nowhere near what I wanted. I told Claude Code that I wanted it to look like a dense jungle but the scale is all wrong. I think he was trying to keep it realistic with the scene, but remember this is art. They don't have to be proportionate. I want them to be big enough for the user to understand that's a jungle." "One thing I think we might not be able to pull off well is the mountain. We can actually scrap that idea and put a monument or something in its place, something spectacular. That'd be cool. Not another pyramid." "Remember he's still working and we don't wanna overwrite anything. We don't know what he's committed or not and pushed. Go ahead and update everything and give me the prompt, please."
        - In exact terms:
        - **The nature area at the left becomes a dense jungle, out of scale on purpose.** This replaces "the nature area does look good" above. Its trees are large enough that a visitor knows it for a jungle at a glance. Nothing on this world has to be in proportion.
        - **A second natural area on the opposite side,** the right, which looks completely different from the jungle.
        - **The mountain is dropped.** A monument stands in its place: spectacular, and not another pyramid.
        - **The rest of Fable's plan stands as put to him:** the city pulled back from the edge at the same density, bare ground at the rim, polished metal used boldly, the craft made good or removed, more kinds of building. It is items 19 to 27 of the agreement below.
        - **Nothing of Claude Code's work in hand is to be overwritten.**
        - Not in the plan, because he did not answer it: Fable's note that the six streams from the torn face are too even. They are part of the planet, which he likes, and are left as they are.
- **His verdict on the finished surface: no. The planet stays, the surface is made again in an art style that matches the site (Nov 15, 01:43).** From two stills of the build Claude Code called the finished version, kept as `reference/studies/59-hero-the-finished-surface-he-turned-down-at-rest.jpg` and `-whole.jpg`. "I still don't love it. The jungle looks bad and is way too dense. The sand dunes look bad. There is a bunch of empty concrete looking space. The design of the city isn't great. It doesn't feel like a single piece of art. A lot of things are fighting one another. And it may not even match with the rest of the site well." "I love the exploded planet idea, let's keep that. The bottom part and the core look good as well. But I think we need to update the art style of the 3D model to better match the rest of the site and completely rebuild the surface of the planet."
    - In exact terms:
    - **Kept:** the planet torn in half, its torn underside, its core, the debris, the streams from the torn face. Nothing he said touches the sky, the moon, the glow or the layout.
    - **Turned down, all of it:** what stands on the top of the dome. The jungle, the dunes, the bare ground round the city, the city's buildings, and with them the pyramid, the Sun disc and the craft. Items 19 to 26 of the agreement below described that surface. They no longer describe what he wants.
    - **What is wrong with it, in his words:** it is not one piece of art, its parts fight one another, and it may not match the rest of the site.
    - **What is asked for:** a new art style for the 3D model that matches the site, and the surface built again from nothing in that style.
    - The art style is not chosen. Fable's reading of why the surface failed, and a direction, are put to him in the chat. Nothing more is built on the surface until he picks.
- **He likes the goldsmith direction and wants a derby before he accepts it (Nov 15, 01:48).** "I like your recommendation a lot, but I don't know if I want to just accept the very first thing. Can you give me like two more options to choose from? I would love a derby to be able to get an idea of each look."
    - In exact terms: nothing is picked. Three looks for the surface are to be built so he can see each: Fable's recommendation (the surface as one piece of metalwork in dark bronze and gold) and two others.
    - Fable began the derby at `/lab/surface-looks` (Study 31) with the three looks on one shared plan, rings and avenues of towers round the spire, so that only the look would differ.
- **His look at the first still of that derby: the shape is wrong too (Nov 15, 02:08).** He stopped the work. "I do not like that the shape of this city is pretty much the same as the one we're replacing. The shape is a thing I dislike. It's like you're using the same layout."
    - In exact terms: the turned down surface was a spire at the middle with towers packed round it, tallest at the middle and falling to the edge, a mound of buildings over the whole top of the dome. The first look of the derby had that same shape in new materials. **The shape itself is turned down, not only the materials.** A new surface has a different composition: what stands where, how much stands at all, and what the skyline is.
    - Fable's miss: holding the layout the same across the three looks, on purpose, when the layout is one of the things he wants changed.
    - What is still settled and is kept in every option: a spire with rings round it on the center line, its tip under the buttons, and the stream leaving the core under it.
    - Three shapes are put to him in the chat as silhouettes, each with the material that belongs to it. Nothing more is built until he answers.
- **His pick of shapes: A and C. The planet comes first, and it still has a living city and nature (Nov 15, 02:16).** From three silhouettes put to him in the chat (A · Rings round one spire, B · Canyons cut into the planet, C · A burst of shards). "The 2 I like the most are A and C. I like those. Figuring out what kind of planet it is and then figuring out how the city lays on top, that may give us a better world than building a city on an [undesigned] planet." "That being said I still want a city. It needs to feel alive, like a population lives there. While also feeling like a world with nature."
    - In exact terms:
    - **A and C are kept. B is not.**
    - **The order of design changes.** First: what kind of planet this is. Then: how a city lies on a planet of that kind. Not a city set down on a blank dome.
    - **There is still a city on it,** and it reads as lived in by a population. The silhouettes showed almost none, so both get one.
    - **The world has nature as well as a city.** In the site's own colors, as ruled before.
    - "Undesigned" is Fable's reading of a word his dictation wrote as "undeceived".
    - Fable's two planets, put to him in the chat and his to change. **A, a river world:** the planet is dark bronze banded in rings, its water is the stream's light, which rises in a spring under the spire and runs out in rivers to the tear. The city follows the rivers. Fields, groves and pools lie between them. One grand spire with great gold rings stands over the spring. **C, a crystal world:** the planet grows crystal, giant shards of dark glass thrust out of it at angles, lopsided. The city is built into and between them. Groves of small crystals, veins and pools of light. The tallest shard is the spire, with rings.
    - Both are built as a lit derby at `/lab/surface-looks`, on the real planet.
- **Two worlds, as built (Nov 15). Study 31, at `/lab/surface-looks`. Waiting on his pick. Nothing of it is a rule.**
    - Both stand on the real planet as built (its torn underside, core and debris), at the page's size and from the page's side, with a closer view of the crown and of the edge.
    - **A · A river world.** The ground is dark bronze turned in rings round the top. A spring of the stream's light lies under the spire and five rivers run from it to the tear, with a brook down the middle. No river runs straight at the visitor: the middle of the picture is a valley of field plots, some flooded, with a lake, a village and groves. The city stands along the rivers, tallest part way down each and low round the spring, so there is no mound. Its buildings are dark bronze with lit windows, capped in gold and roofed in copper: needles, slabs, drums, halls with colonnades, houses. Lamps and bridges along the rivers. One spire, with three great rings of polished gold round it, each carrying a row of lights that goes slowly round.
    - **C · A crystal world.** The ground is black. Eighteen great shards of amber glass stand out of it at angles in a lopsided burst, each with smaller ones at its foot. The tallest is the spire, straight, with three copper rings. People live in the great shards: windows in neighbourhoods, copper decks round them, bridges with lamps between them. A town of dark houses with copper roofs stands at their feet and along seven veins of light, which branch and pool. Fields of small crystals run out to the tear.
    - **Fable's calls, each his to overturn:** the planet's water is the stream's light. Nature is fields, groves, a lake and ponds on A, and crystal fields, veins and pools on C. The spire keeps its place and its height on both. The glass is amber and lit from its foot, not dark.
    - **Short of the bar, said on the page:** the buildings are simple forms, the trees are plain, and nothing moves but the lights on A's rings. C's shards read as amber stone as much as glass. Neither has been seen on a real graphics card or by a fresh reviewer.
    - **What a pick settles:** the kind of planet, the shape of what stands on it, and the materials. Not the detail. The picked world goes to Claude Code with this study as the basic version.
- **His look at the two worlds: a pause. Neither is good enough, and both read as a city on a floating platform (Nov 15, 10:23).** "Let's pause for a moment. These aren't coming out as good as I would've hoped. I guess my favorite one is option A here but it's still not amazing. I'm looking through images now to try to find some references that might help us out. One thing I can say is all of these look like a city on a floating platform. It doesn't look like it's its own little world, but maybe we don't have enough space to make our own world and maybe that's why this isn't working."
    - In exact terms:
    - **Nothing is picked.** A is the better of the two and is not good enough. C is not chosen.
    - **The fault he names is in the planet's shape as seen, not in what stands on it:** every version so far, built or studied, reads as a city on a floating platform and not as a small world of its own.
    - **His question, not a ruling:** whether the hero has room for a world at all.
    - **He is gathering reference pictures.** Nothing is built until he sends them.
    - Fable's reading, given in the chat and not a rule: it reads as a platform because the planet is one. He asked twice for a flatter dome ("The sphere is a bit rounder than I would've hoped", then "you can really see the grade of the planet"), and it is now a cap 1320 across and 165 tall, seen from its edge, with both of its ends inside the screen. That is the outline of a disc. Two ways out were put to him in words: a rounder, smaller ball, or a planet wider than the screen whose horizon runs off both sides.
    - Fable's miss in world A, said to him: its terraced fields go against his ruling of Nov 14 that the farms and the terraced gardens are dropped, and its city is small where he ruled that the city is large.
- **What is wrong with world A, in his words, and what he is after (Nov 15, 10:32).** "I don't know if I want to make it smaller. I feel like the width of it is perfect." "I don't like how the buildings group around the rivers. I don't like how bare and [tiled] the ground is. There's very sparse trees and the trees that are there are repetitive. It doesn't feel like an actual landscape. It's like here's one city in a single biome, where I'm looking for diversity. My original idea was to have some type of nature section, a forest, and then maybe like a desert. But I definitely don't want the platform to feel like it's made out of concrete or man-made, which is kind of the vibe I'm getting looking at this right now. It doesn't look like it's an actual planet that's been partly destroyed. It looks like it's some type of man-made object with some kind of energy core inside of it." "Some of it may be the actual base. I think the core looks really good on Claude Code's version, but the [jagged part] of the bottom could probably be better, more planet like." "Once we actually finish the finalized design of this, the big chunks of debris should have artwork that matches the artwork of the actual non-destroyed part of the planet, such as if it has trees on it, it should match the trees." "I don't love the way the buildings look. I don't love how the buildings are grouped in rows. You really can't tell that these are rivers at all. It's super empty and looks like a spaceship. The rings are a bit too big and I wouldn't look at this and assume that they're part of the building. It's just not good."
    - In exact terms:
    - **The width stays.** About 1320 across. He does not want the planet smaller.
    - **The ground is natural, never made.** Now, in world A: a plate of turned bronze with rectangular plots, which reads as metal, concrete, a spaceship. To be: the ground of a real planet. Nothing about the planet's body reads as a man-made object with a power source in it. This turns down the goldsmith ground he liked in words on Nov 15.
    - **A landscape with more than one biome.** Now: one city and one kind of ground. To be: different kinds of land on one world. His own first idea, restated: a forest, and perhaps a desert, beside the city.
    - **Trees:** many more, and not one tree repeated.
    - **The city:** not in rows, and not strung along rivers. He does not like the buildings' look either.
    - **The rivers of light** do not read as rivers.
    - **The rings** are too big and do not read as part of the spire.
    - **The planet's underside is open again.** The core is right as Claude Code built it. The torn rock under the world could be better and more like a planet's. This changes "the planet stays" above for the torn rock only.
    - **The debris matches the surface.** When the surface is final, the large pieces that broke away carry the same land as the top: the same trees, the same ground.
    - Still paused. He is gathering references.
- **The prompt about the crash was not sent (Nov 15).** He held it so that Claude Code would not keep working on a thing that may be replaced. The crash on his device (the page goes black, reloads, and Safari says a problem repeatedly occurred) is still not looked at. It leads the next prompt to Claude Code.
- **His proposal: a clean slate for the hero's world (Nov 15, 10:36). Put as a question, and Fable agrees. Not yet a go.** "I never liked the first city layout you handed me and I gave that to Claude Code and he built that. Even though we put in the prompt that he should do something different, he still used that same layout, and all of these updates we've been giving him and what you've been giving me all kind of work on that same layout. I think what we need to do is completely forget about the other city for a moment, including the core, the debris field and everything. Just put it off to the side and let's build a derby for a completely new 3D model with the same idea as the exploded planet, the debris field and the core that perfectly ties into our stream, and start over fresh with that clean slate. What do you think about that?"
    - In exact terms:
    - **Set aside, all of it, and not deleted:** the world as built (`src/pieces/skycity/`: its dome, its torn underside, its core, its debris and everything on top) and Study 31's two worlds, which stand on that same dome.
    - **Kept as the idea only:** a planet that has been torn apart, a debris field, and a core that the site's stream comes out of.
    - **Asked for:** a derby of completely new 3D models of that idea, each designed whole from nothing, none of them starting from the old plan.
    - What the record shows, which bears him out: the plan of the city (a spire at the middle with rings of towers round it) was never put to him as a choice. It came from the outlines in Fable's wireframe, went into Claude Code's plan, and every round since stood on it, Study 31 included, which reused the built dome and its torn edge.
    - Fable's view, given in the chat: yes. With three conditions so the fresh start does not end where the last ones did. His references come first. Each entry in the derby is a whole planet, not a surface. And how the derby is made is decided before it is made (`BRIEF.md`, Open 5).
- **Thinking it through together: no Blender, pictures from Gemini as the reference, and what he sees in his head (Nov 15, 10:45). Thoughts, and he says so: none of this is a rule.** "The goal here isn't to make what I'm envisioning. It's just to make something really cool... I don't want you to get stuck in 'we have to have this, this and this'. I want to work together on this as partners, so if something sounds stupid or doesn't seem like it's going to work well, let me know."
    - **Blender: he does not want it.** "I don't know if I want to make it in Blender. I haven't done that before... never a web asset, and to be honest the game assets you've made in Blender haven't been incredible." Open 5 is not ruled for the site. For this piece Fable drops the recommendation: it is made in code.
    - **He has found no reference pictures.** His idea, which Fable agrees with: have Gemini make pictures from a prompt, "just to see what it comes up with". The prompt Fable wrote for him is in `LOG.md`.
    - **Where Egypt came from:** the Work section. The cards, the rods and the Sun stones reminded him of it, "even though that's probably more like [Mayan] or something". **He wants a pyramid on the planet, in the city and not out in the desert,** because it would look cool.
    - **The buildings are a city of the future, plainly.** Not alien, and not a future version of Egypt's buildings. He pointed to Star Trek because anyone would look and say "that's a city from the future".
    - **The ground is not bare, and there is diversity.**
    - **Forest has not worked.** "Every iteration of a forest or jungle we try to put on the surface hasn't come out well. They've always been too dense and read really off."
    - **Undecided, his own question:** wild nature areas, or parks inside a giant city, with the city going round a desert of dunes.
    - Fable's view, given in the chat: play to what code does well. A large city of the future round a desert of dunes, the pyramid in the city, trees as parks and avenues inside the city and no wild forest, broken rock toward the tear.
- **Two concept pictures from Gemini, and his first look (Nov 15, 10:49).** Made from Fable's prompt, kept as `reference/concept/01-gemini-a-floating-slab.jpg` and `02-gemini-a-half-planet-with-its-core-open.jpg`. They are pictures made by an image model, for agreeing a look. Neither is a plan to copy. "I think the bottom jagged area actually looks pretty good on these. The city could probably be a bit more grander though, but definitely not a bad place."
    - In exact terms: the torn rock under the world in both pictures is the kind he wants. The city in both is too modest: it should be grander. As a starting place they are good. Nothing is picked.
    - Fable's reading, given in the chat: the second is the stronger, because the round body under it makes it a planet and the opening shows the core whole. Both are seen from well above, about 25 degrees, so the land is seen laid out: the built world is seen from 4.5 degrees and shows only a skyline. That view was Fable's call and never his ruling, and it is the first thing to change. Against the brief in the pictures: the first is a floating slab, which he has turned down, both have flat tops, the second has blue and green on its sides, and the trees are green.
- **The world is tallest at its middle (Nov 15, 10:52).** "This is going to be our hero asset, so I naturally want it to be at its highest point in the center. That will go up directly in the center of the content on the hero and would look really good. So we still need some type of spire or bigger building in the middle, and the skyline should kind of come down diagonally from there on each side. Not a perfect diagonal. We don't want to lose the realism, but naturally that's how it should work."
    - In exact terms: the tallest thing on the world, a spire or one greater building, stands on the hero's center line under his words. From it the skyline falls away to the left and to the right, unevenly, as a real city's does.
    - **How this sits with what he turned down at 02:08,** said to him in the chat: that was also tallest at the middle. Fable's reading of the difference, his to correct: what he disliked was a round heap of like towers ringed evenly about the spire and covering the whole top. What he asks for now is a skyline with one peak, uneven, on a world whose land is seen from above with a desert and parks beside the city.
- **The third concept picture, and what is off in it (Nov 15, 10:55).** Gemini's edit of the second, kept as `reference/concept/03-gemini-the-spire-at-the-middle-and-a-grander-city.jpg`. "What still feels off to me about this one is, for one, it moved the pyramid to the desert area, and the desert area has shrunk significantly. The dunes are a lot smaller. This kind of takes away from that. Also, the core is pretty much all the way up directly under the city. I don't like the way that looks either. I kind of like the idea of there being much more jaggedness and broken earth there, and the core further down."
    - In exact terms:
    - **The pyramid is in the city,** among the buildings. In this picture it stands on the sand.
    - **The desert is large, with large dunes.** In this picture it is a small corner with low dunes.
    - **The core hangs well below the surface,** with a deep body of jagged, broken earth between the city and it. In this picture the core is just under the city's ground.
    - He did not fault the spire at the middle, the grander city, the lit windows or the trees in autumn colors.
- **More on the third picture: the city is too small, the spire is right, and one ring (Nov 15, 10:57).** "The city kind of looks off, like the buildings aren't as big as I would hope them to be. The [spire] looks great. It would be cool if [it] had like a single ring at the top, maybe tilted, kind of like Saturn's. But the rest of the city looks a bit small. It's definitely dense enough. It just isn't as grand as I would've hoped."
    - In exact terms: the spire in the third picture is the spire he wants. One ring round it near its top, tilted, as Saturn's is: his idea, offered as "it would be cool". The city is dense enough and its buildings are too small beside the planet: they are larger, so the city is grand.
    - He is on Gemini's free plan with few messages left. Fable's view: a new chat with one whole prompt and the third picture attached for its look only, since what is left to change is the picture's structure, which an edit does poorly.
- **The fourth concept picture: the top is right, the tear is not (Nov 15, 10:59).** From a new chat, kept as `reference/concept/04-gemini-the-top-he-likes-with-a-ring-and-large-dunes.jpg`. "I think the top part looks a lot better. The core's positioning is a bit better as well, but the 3D and detailed jaggedness is missing. It looked much better on the other iteration."
    - In exact terms: what stands on the top in the fourth picture is the best so far. The core's place, lower in the planet, is better. The torn rock is worse: in the fourth it is a cracked wall with the core set on it, and in the second and third it is a deep hollow of broken rock with real depth, which is what he wants.
- **The target so far, gathered from the four pictures (Nov 15). Fable's summary of what he has said, his to correct. Pictures made by an image model set the look. None is a plan to copy.**
    1. **A clean slate.** A new model of the same idea. The built world and Study 31 are set aside, not deleted.
    2. **The planet:** a deep half sphere of natural warm rock, about as wide as now. Seen from the front and from well above, so its land is seen laid out. Its top curves and is not a flat table.
    3. **The tear:** the front torn open into a deep hollow of jagged broken earth with real depth, as in pictures 2 and 3. Never a cracked wall.
    4. **The core:** a ball of the stream's light hanging low in that hollow, well under the surface, as placed in picture 4. The stream falls from it.
    5. **The city:** of the future, plainly. Dense, with buildings that are large beside the planet, lit windows in the thousands. Grander than any picture so far.
    6. **The spire:** as in pictures 3 and 4, on the hero's center line, the tallest thing by far, with one thin ring near its top, tilted as Saturn's is. The skyline falls from it to each side, unevenly.
    7. **The pyramid:** in the city, among the buildings, to one side of the spire, as in picture 4.
    8. **The desert:** a large stretch of the surface with tall dunes and sharp crests, as in pictures 2 and 4. Nothing built on it.
    9. **Trees:** in parks and along avenues inside the city, in autumn colors. No wild forest.
    10. **Debris:** pieces of the planet round the tear, the large ones carrying the same land as the top.
    11. **Colors:** the site's. No blue, no green, no white.
    12. **Made in code.** Not in Blender.
    13. **Shadows carry the realism.** One low light with real cast shadows, deep and exact, on the dunes, in the torn rock and on the city.
- **Go on a grey blockout, and the shadows are what make it real (Nov 15, 11:01).** "Sure, I'm willing to give it a shot. There is one thing I want to point out. The moon in our hero looks exceptionally realistic, and I think we're doing that with the way the shadows look. And I get the same vibe when I look at the sand dunes on this, and all the crevices and cracks in the [jagged rock] in this. What makes them look ultra realistic is the shadows. We should absolutely make sure that we're capturing that realism with the shadows exceptionally well."
    - In exact terms: Fable builds a plain grey blockout of the new world in real 3D, to settle its shape, the camera and how it sits under his words, before any detail. He approves that, and Claude Code then builds the look to the four pictures.
    - **A ruling for the build: shadows carry the realism.** On the dunes, in every crevice and crack of the torn rock, and on the city, as they do on the hero's moon. One low light with real cast shadows, deep and exact, is not a finish to add later: it is the thing that makes the world read as real. It is item 13 of the target above.
- **The grey blockout, as built (Nov 15). Study 32, at `/lab/hero-blockout`. Waiting on his look. Nothing of it is a rule until he approves it.**
    - A new model, with nothing taken from the old plan, in plain grey, lit by one low moon with real shadows and by the core's own light in the hollow. Under his words, laid out as the hero is.
    - **The planet:** 1320 across, as before. Its top is a gentle dome that falls 118 from its middle to its rim. Under the rim its body is a bowl 560 deep.
    - **The camera:** from the front and 12 degrees above, with 8 and 16 behind a switch. The built world is seen from 4.5. Fable told him the pictures were seen from about 25: measured, they are about 10 to 15.
    - **At rest on a 1080 screen:** the spire with its ring and the taller buildings, with the pyramid and the dunes coming into the foot of the screen. The land, the tear and the core come up on the scroll.
    - **The spire:** 330 tall, with one ring 132 across near its top, tipped 17 degrees. Nothing else stands over 205.
    - **The city:** blocks 80 apart over all the land that is not desert. A tall block is one tower and a low one is four small buildings. Tallest round the spire and falling to each side unevenly. An open court in front of the spire, and an avenue lined with trees from it to where the ground is torn away.
    - **The pyramid:** 196 across and 122 tall, in the city at the left of the spire, in a court ringed with trees.
    - **The desert:** the right third of the land, coming round toward the visitor, with fifteen crescent dunes up to 48 tall.
    - **The tear:** the front of the planet opened into a hollow about 600 across and the planet's whole depth, with 100 to 230 of rock left between the city and its roof, no floor, broken into blocks and beds, with cracks running out from it.
    - **The core:** a ball 168 across, 436 under the top of the planet, which is about two thirds of the way down. The stream falls from it.
    - **Debris:** seventeen pieces round and under the tear, two of them carrying a piece of the land.
    - **Short of what he asked for, said to him:** the tear is jagged at a middling scale. It does not yet have the great angular slabs of pictures 2 and 3. The paving near the tear at the front left is open. Every building, tree and piece of debris is a plain stand in.
    - **What approving it settles:** the planet's outline and depth, the camera, the spire's height, the sizes of the city, the pyramid and the desert and where each lies, where the core hangs, and how much shows at rest. **What it does not:** any material, any detail, anything that moves.
- **His look at the grey blockout: a good direction, not nearly there, and large changes wanted (Nov 15, 12:25).** With two pictures: his favorite of Gemini's for the tear, kept as `reference/concept/05-his-favorite-for-the-tear-and-the-core.jpg` (the third picture, cropped), and the blockout with his red marks, kept as `reference/studies/61-hero-blockout-his-marks-where-city-replaces-desert.jpg`. "On the default hero view, so what it's going to look like before the user scrolls down, you can see quite a bit of desert on the right-hand side. This is causing [an asymmetry], which I don't necessarily love. I think we should add in some city there." "I don't love the perspective of the broken part of the planet. If you look at it, it looks like a piece of the planet's been cut off, and the rest of it looks fine and smooth. This is not what it's supposed to look like. It's supposed to look like the planet has been ripped apart. Imagine ripping someone's head off and their spinal cord and everything's torn, dangling right there." Of his favorite: "This one demonstrates how I want the core area to look, but it still has smoothness on the sides, which I don't necessarily want. It's fine if some of it is smooth, but it shouldn't end in a smooth [edge]. It should always look like it's been ripped apart. Some sides much bigger than the others. Cracked and gnarled." Of his marks: "There's a row of sand dunes in the far back, and all the way to the left side at the desert area, that I think we can [put] more buildings there, to increase the size of the city. There also isn't much in terms of nature here. Yes, we have the desert on the right-hand side, but a lot of the city is bland and empty. What are we planning to put on the bottom floor? Are they roads? What should it look like?" "I'm not necessarily looking for small changes at this point. We have a good direction, but it's not almost there. It still needs quite a bit of work. The desert area is too big, and the city could definitely use some work, especially with some of the positioning of its buildings. They seem like they're in rows. This is a sphere, so we're able to put stuff on the back side that pokes up to give even more perspective of how big the city is."
    - In exact terms:
    - **At rest the picture is balanced.** Now: before any scroll, buildings stand at the left of the spire and open desert at its right. To be: city on both sides of the spire at rest.
    - **The planet is ripped apart, not cut into.** Now: a whole smooth bowl with one hollow taken out of its front. To be: torn everywhere it ends. Nothing ends on a smooth edge. Some smooth skin may remain, but every edge of it is broken. The torn sides are uneven, some far larger than others, cracked and gnarled, with pieces left hanging. The rock round the core is as in picture 5: great angular slabs.
    - **The desert is smaller.** Now: the right third of the land. To be: less, with buildings where its far row of dunes and its left edge are now. This changes "The desert is large" in the target above: it keeps tall dunes and gives up ground.
    - **The city is larger and is not in rows.** Now: a square grid, so the buildings line up. To be: no rows from the page's view, more buildings, and buildings on the far side of the planet whose tops stand up behind the rest, so the city's size is felt.
    - **The city is not bland or empty, and has nature in it.** Now: bare paving between the buildings. To be decided: what the ground is. He asked, and Fable's proposal is put to him in the chat.
    - **Large changes, not small ones.**
    - He asked for a detailed plan and whether to go back to Gemini. Fable's plan is in the chat and in `LOG.md`. Nothing is changed until he says go.
- **More context for the blockout, and go (Nov 15, 12:36).** "Yes, you understood what I said exactly. Let me give you a little bit more context. So at rest on the default view before the user scrolls, I want them to see this futuristic city. I don't necessarily want them to see the landscapes of the city or the desert. As they scroll down, they get to see more of the planet. They see the desert, the vegetation, and so on. But as they keep scrolling, that's when they see that this planet has suffered something catastrophic. There also should be tons of flying debris, orbiting and [tidally] locked ones, kind of similar to what we have now in Claude Code." "Yes, you're good to go ahead and start working."
    - In exact terms:
    - **The hero is seen in three steps as the page scrolls.** At rest, before any scroll: the city of the future, its spire and its skyline, and no desert and no open land. On the first scroll: more of the planet, laid out, with the desert and the trees and parks. Further down: the catastrophe, which is the torn rock, the core and what broke away.
