    - So the desert and the open land lie on the near half of the top, toward the visitor, which is the part under the foot of the screen at rest. The far half, and all of the skyline, is city.
    - **Debris, a great deal of it.** Some in orbit round the world, some holding its place with one face to the core, as the world Claude Code built has it. It belongs to the third step: none of it shows at rest.
    - **Go** on the plan for the blockout's second round, as put to him at 12:25 (`LOG.md`). His five parts stand as written, with "balanced at rest" now meaning the city alone at rest.
- **The blockout's second round, as built (Nov 15). Study 32, at `/lab/hero-blockout`. Waiting on his look. Nothing of it is a rule until he approves it.** Stills in `reference/studies/62-*`.
    - Built to the plan he said go on and to his three steps on the scroll. Still plain grey, lit by one low moon with real shadows and by the core's own light.
    - **The three steps, on a 16:9 screen.** At rest: the spire with its ring, towers on both sides of it and behind it, the pyramid at the left. No sand and no open land. On the first scroll: the near half of the top laid out, with the avenue, the parks, the low city and the desert. Further down: the torn rock, the core and what broke away.
    - **On a screen taller than 16:9 a strip of the near land shows at rest.** On a 16:10 screen that is the far edge of the sand and the trees along it (`62-hero-blockout-second-round-at-rest-on-a-taller-screen.jpg`). It follows from "a taller screen shows more of the world" in "The hero, the city at rest" below. Fable's recommendation, his to rule on: the first screen ends at the same place of the world on every screen, and a taller screen gets more sky over the spire.
    - **The planet, ripped.** The rock is in great pieces about 186 across, each kept whole or gone whole, so every face is a face where two pieces parted and nothing ends on a smooth edge. Its underside hangs to about 650 under the top at the visitor's right, which the moon lights, and to about 430 at the left. The hollow round the core is about 600 across, open to the visitor and open below, under a crust about 100 to 150 thick that the city stands on. Long pieces hang from the torn underside, up to about 300 long, and none between the core and the visitor. The bowl's skin survives in patches, split by cracks that open and close along their length. The land's edge is bitten back all the way round, most over the hollow: the land is 81% of the whole top.
    - **The city.** Nine districts, each with its own street angle, so nothing lines up from the page's view. Fourteen boulevards, which are the lines where two districts meet, each lined with trees with a line of the stream's light down its middle, and the avenue from the spire's court to where the ground is torn away. 49 towers of six plain forms, round the spire and across the far half, which is raised so tops stand behind tops. The tallest, beside the spire, is 198 against the spire's 330, and the skyline falls from there to each side, unevenly: a second, lower peak at the right of about 147, and about 136 at the left behind the pyramid. 600 small buildings round courtyards with a tree in each, under 75 tall but ten. About fifty of them, at the land's broken edge, lean toward the break.
    - **The ground.** Streets, boulevards and the two courts, as darker and lighter grey, drawn to the land's broken edge.
    - **Trees and parks.** A great park at the front left, a long one beside the avenue, one at the right, a belt where the city meets the sand, a ring round each court, and a tree at each block's corners on the near half. About 1650 trees.
    - **The desert.** At the front right, 11% of the land, where the first round's was a third. Ten dunes up to 46 tall, the low ones nearest the city.
    - **What broke away.** 277 pieces. Seven great ones that still carry a piece of the city or of the desert. 52 that hold their place with their narrow end to the core. 134 in two belts, one round the tear and one under the planet. 84 small ones falling away under the core along the stream. None shows at rest. **None moves:** the blockout is judged from stills in the web chat, and how each kind moves is written in `plan.ts` for the build.
    - **Kept from the first round:** the width, the camera at 12 degrees with 8 and 16 behind a switch, the spire and its ring, the pyramid at the left, where the core hangs. The host is taller, 1480, for what broke away.
    - **Short of what he asked for, said to him:** the towers are plain boxes and drums, so the skyline has the right outline and is not yet grand. The left of the torn rock is in the planet's own shadow and reads dark. The right hangs lower than the left by about 220, which is plain and not extreme. In a still the belts cannot be told from the rest of what broke away. The low city at the front left is thin in places. The rock's faces are large and flat with nothing at a smaller scale.
    - **What approving it settles:** the planet's torn shape, how deep each side hangs, the hollow, the city's plan and skyline, where the desert and the parks lie and how large each is, how much of what breaks away there is and where, and what each of the three steps shows. **What it does not:** any material, any detail, anything that moves.
- **His look at the second round: a major improvement, and not where he wants it (Nov 15, 14:25).** With the whole view marked in green and red, kept as `reference/studies/62-hero-blockout-second-round-his-marks-city-along-the-avenue-and-a-smaller-desert.jpg`. "So the major improvement, I do have some feedback though." "Green shows where I think the city should go. Currently the main road is actually being cut off by something. I think the city should continue onwards down that road alongside of it, and if not the city some type of intelligent civilization structures." "Next is the desert. I'd like it to be a bit smaller. I've outlined it in red. There's also a part of the cracked earth that is impeding on the desert area. This makes it look really bad, and it's covering up potentially the best sand dune in the desert." "There are still some blank spots on top where nothing is at. I'm not saying we have to fill the whole thing up perfectly, but [it] needs to at least have something on the ground, a reason why it looks like that." "Also don't like the vegetation or the trees that are surrounding the edge of the desert. I think that looks really bad. It might be better just to take that all the way to the city." "We have no diversity in our vegetation whatsoever. They all look about the same. I think we should probably do a much more higher fidelity rendering of the top layer of the 3D model. That way I can get an idea of the detail that's gonna be on the cities, the textures that are gonna be used, the different types of terrains. This needs to be signed off before we pass it to Claude Code." "The bottom part of [the] 3D model looks a lot better, but that's also hard to judge without actual color being on it. I don't understand the depth or how the shadows will look." "It's definitely not where I want it. What do you recommend? Go ahead and hand off to Claude Code after we make those few iterations and see a finalized polished version of it, or should we do a more higher fidelity version [here] and nail down a lot more things."
    - In exact terms:
    - **The avenue is lined with city on both sides, all the way down.** Now: a long park runs down its left side and trees and sand down its right, and its near end is hidden where the land is bitten away. To be: buildings, or other works of a civilization, along both sides from the spire's court to the land's broken edge, with the road itself seen the whole way.
    - **The desert is smaller again.** Now: 11% of the land, reaching the avenue. To be: his red line. Its left edge moves to the right, away from the avenue, and what it gives up is city.
    - **No broken ground cuts into the desert.** Now: a cracked and shifted plate of the land's edge lies across the front of the desert and across its largest dune. To be: the desert whole, with that dune seen entire.
    - **No bare patches on top.** Now: open ground with nothing on it in several places, most at the front left. To be: something on every stretch of ground, or a plain reason for it being open. It need not all be filled.
    - **No trees round the desert.** Now: a belt of trees where the city meets the sand. To be: the sand runs up to the city itself.
    - **The vegetation has kinds.** Now: every tree is the same ball. To be: plainly different kinds.
    - **A ruling: the look of the top is signed off by him before anything goes to Claude Code.** He wants the top at a far higher fidelity, so that he can judge the detail of the city, the textures and the kinds of terrain.
    - **The torn rock is better, and he cannot judge it in grey:** not its depth, and not how its shadows will look.
    - He asked which way to go: a few more rounds here and then Claude Code, or a higher fidelity version here first. Fable's recommendation is in the chat and in `LOG.md`. Nothing is changed until he answers.
- **Go on the higher fidelity round, here (Nov 15, 14:30).** "Yes, go ahead and make these updates." In exact terms: Fable makes his six changes to the top and takes the same model into color in one round, in the web chat: the top with real materials, lit windows, kinds of tree and kinds of ground, and the torn rock in color with the moon's shadows and the core's light. Shown at the page's size in its three steps and in close views of the city, the land and the rock. He signs that off before anything goes to Claude Code. It still does not move.
- **The third round, in color, as built (Nov 15). Study 32, at `/lab/hero-blockout`. Waiting on his look. Nothing of it is a rule until he signs it off.** Stills in `reference/studies/63-*`.
    - **His six changes, as made.**
    - **The avenue:** thirteen halls down both sides, each with a row of columns to the road, from the spire's court to the land's broken edge. Seventeen obelisks with gold tips between them, and a gate post where it ends. No tree stands on it. The road is 56 wide and seen the whole way, with the line of light down its middle.
    - **The desert:** 10% of the land, where it was 11% and before that a third. Its left edge stands off the avenue, with city between. It is whole: nothing is bitten out of it and no crack crosses it, and its largest dune stands entire in its middle. Nine dunes.
    - **No trees round the desert:** the sand runs to the buildings' feet.
    - **No bare patches:** ground in the city with nothing built on it is a planted square, with ground cover, bushes and a tree here and there. Bare rock shows only at the land's broken edge.
    - **Kinds of plant:** six. Broad crowns, tall slim poplars, dark cones, palms, low bushes, and pale trees in flower, in autumn colors and no green. About 2000 of them. One kind to a boulevard, palms round the pyramid's court, clipped cones round the spire's, mixed stands in the parks.
    - **Three parks:** the great one at the front left, one at the left beside the pyramid, one at the right. The one that ran down the avenue is gone.
    - **The look.**
    - **The rock:** warm, in beds of differing tone that are not level, darker the deeper it lies, with a paler dusty skin where the planet's own surface survives.
    - **The ground:** paving in slabs, darker streets, paler boulevards and avenue, the two courts paved in rings, planted squares, the parks' grass, sand rippled across the wind.
    - **The buildings:** 706. Stone, stone under copper roofs, bronze clad, and dark glass. Windows lit in neighbourhoods and not evenly. A tall tower ends in a band of light. Gold on domes, caps and crowns.
    - **The spire:** a dark shaft with lit windows, gold collars, the one gold ring, a light at its tip. **The pyramid:** stone in courses under a gold cap.
    - **What broke away:** the same rock. The great pieces carry paving, buildings and trees, or sand.
    - **Close views** of the city, the land and the rock, behind a switch.
    - **Short of the bar, said to him:** the buildings are still plain forms with windows drawn on them. Close up they read as a model of a city and not as a city: no balconies, ledges, bridges or roof works, and one window pattern to a material. The halls are boxes with a row of columns. The trees are a few simple forms. The dark side of the torn rock holds little detail close up. The core is a plain ball. Nothing moves.
    - **What signing it off settles:** the palette of the world, what each thing is made of, how lit the city is, the kinds of plant and of ground, and the plan of the top with his six changes. **What it does not:** the finished detail of the buildings, anything that moves, the night sky.
- **His look at the third round: the top is to be picture 4's, the torn rock is rebuilt to be looked at, and he asked to go over it before anything is built (Nov 15, 17:46).** With picture 4 marked in red and green, kept as `reference/studies/64-picture-4-his-marks-a-larger-jagged-tear-and-buildings-behind-the-dunes.jpg`. Fable's reading of the marks, drawn over the same picture and shown to him, is `reference/studies/64-picture-4-fables-reading-of-his-marks.jpg`. "One thing that I feel like it's severely lacking on the top is the sand dunes. I actually like the top more on the one that Gemini gave us. It's almost perfect. I mean I still want like the golden tops on some of the buildings and so on, but I think the number of buildings it has and the way it's spaced out the buildings is pretty much perfect. I'd love to re-create that exact layout." "[Theirs] is obviously more concaved than ours. Ours look like they're just kinda bunched up and flat on one side, where [theirs] actually has the 3D depth that you would see in the sand dunes, and because the light source is behind the scene off to the right, it's creating that perfect shadow to make them super realistic." "The trees look way better than ours. I'd like to use the exact trees that's on the Gemini layout, but I wanna keep everything off the edge separate from the Gemini layout." Open to adding more of that flat section that I took away before [if] it [really seals] the deal.     - **The top is picture 4's layout.** Now: 706 small buildings in nine districts, an avenue lined with halls, three parks, a small desert. To be: what picture 4 has, where it has it. The spire with its one ring on the center line. About 40 large towers standing apart, in a band behind and beside the spire, falling away to each end. The pyramid in front of them at the left. Trees filling the ground between the towers and the land's front edge. An avenue from the spire to the front edge. The desert at the front right. Gold tops on some of the towers stay.
    - **Buildings behind the dunes.** In picture 4 the skyline stops short of the right end, with only trees at the back of the desert. To be: two or three buildings there, his green marks, so that the skyline runs from one end of the land to the other.
    - **At rest** the visitor sees buildings from end to end, and far enough down to see most of the pyramid.
    - **The dunes.** Now: low mounds that run together, each flat on one side. To be: picture 4's. About six large dunes standing apart, each a crescent with a sharp crest and a deep hollow face, the hollow in full shadow. The moon lights the world from behind and to the right, which is what puts the hollows that face the visitor in shadow.
    - **The trees.** Now: six kinds, each a simple form. To be: picture 4's tree. A full round crown of orange and amber leaves in clumps on a short trunk, planted close in rows and stands.
    - **The pyramid is mirror polished metal.** Now: stone in courses under a gold cap. Fable's call, his to change: the site's gold, not silver.
    - **The colors of the top** are picture 4's: warmer and brighter than the third round. He did not say whether the third round's colors and materials are right.
    - **"Everything off the edge separate."** Fable's reading, his to correct: only what stands on the top is taken from picture 4's layout. The torn rock, the core and what broke away are designed on their own.
    - **The torn rock, the hollow and the core are rebuilt, for how they look.** Now: the break is hard to read, near cannot be told from far, the core's light is a haze, and the rock was shaped to be believed. To be: shaped to be looked at. The break faces the visitor. The rock is in great slabs with deep dark cracks between them, as picture 4's is. The core is a sharp bright ball whose light falls clearly on the faces round it.
    - **His red line, as Fable reads it:** where the near wall of rock ends. Everything under it is torn away, which is about half of the underside, a quarter to a third more than picture 4 has missing. It leaves a great fang of rock on each side of the core and an arch over it, so the core hangs in the open. The round shell at the left and the right no longer ends on a smooth curve: it ends on that jagged line.

To be: a polished gold top on two or three of them, spread across the skyline, and shiny bronze accents on a few others, in the manner of the site's Egyptian pieces: the Sun stones, the obelisks, the Blade finials.

    - **He asked whether to have Gemini combine the two.** Fable's answer: yes. Picture 6 for the top and the debris, picture 7 for the underside, with his two notes. The prompt is in `LOG.md`, Nov 15, "The seventh picture". A combined picture replaces nothing unless it holds both halves: until then pictures 4, 6 and 7 stay the references.

The core is asked for in pale gold with an orange edge, since it came back nearly white and the palette's own rule is that hot parts go to pale gold and never to white.
- **Craters on the right of the planet, something for the left, and the smaller streams (Nov 15, 18:35).** "Couple updates I wanna make to it, both having to do with the flat part. On the right side I would like to put craters on it, with the same effect that the moon in our hero has. It looks ultra realistic, and similar to how the sand dunes are utilizing the shadow as well. I just want there to be some craters on the right side, mainly because the light source will hit [them] perfectly and they will look awesome. On the left side I kinda wanna do something else. I don't know what to put there. What would look good? Maybe like super realistic 3D cracks, but I'm open to other options as well. I feel like that handles the blandness." "There is one other thing that we're missing in this. Earlier in concepts I wanted there to be a few smaller streams of the inner stream coming down. You might have to go look in old screenshots or in the GitHub to see what I'm talking about, but the current version made in Claude Code has it where it has multiple streams coming down. I feel like it just gives it life if that energy is kind of leaking from various points in the mantle of the planet, versus just having the giant core there. What are your thoughts on that?"
    - In exact terms:
    - **"The flat part"** is the smooth outer surface of the planet that survives at the left and at the right of the opening, as picture 7 has it. Now: plain weathered rock, which he finds bland.
    - **The right side has craters. A ruling.** Impact craters of several sizes, as real relief and not as a picture laid on the rock, lit by the moon from the upper right so that each rim throws a shadow across its floor. It is the same thing that makes the hero's moon and the dunes read as real.
    - **The left side gets something else, and he asked what.** His thought is deep 3D cracks. Fable's recommendation, put to him and not ruled: cracks lit from inside. Deep fractures that run out from the tear and branch, with the core's light showing in them, brightest at the tear and gone further out. The reason: the moon is at the upper right, so the left side is in shadow, and a crack with no light in it or on it would not be seen there. Passed over by Fable: plain unlit cracks, and the skin peeled off in plates to show the layers under it.
    - **A few smaller streams leave the torn rock.** He asked for Fable's thoughts. It is already written: "Streams of the stream's own light leave the torn face at several places, and the main one leaves the core" (The hero, the city at rest, item 7), and the world Claude Code built has six of them (`reference/studies/59-hero-the-finished-surface-he-turned-down-whole.jpg`). Fable left them out of the three prompts for Gemini, which was a miss. Fable's view, put to him: yes, and not as the built world has them. There they are six of one size, evenly spaced and of one length, so they read as legs the world stands on. To be: three or four, thin, each a different length and brightness, each leaving from a crack or from under a hanging piece, some nearer and some further back, all far fainter than the main stream, and none of them running on down the page.
    - **Fable's additions, each said to him as his to turn down:** the torn edge cuts through one of the large craters, which shows the craters are older than the break. The smaller streams at the left leave from the ends of the lit cracks, so the cracks and the streams are one idea.

Picture 6 is no longer a reference for anything.

One moon, the one already in the sky at the upper right, and the world's light comes from where it is drawn, behind the world and to the right. No second, artificial light from another direction: shadows that disagree with a moon the visitor can see read as false. If the dunes and craters need the light a little higher or further round, the moon in the sky is moved to match, not the other way about. The cursor's light stays as a glint on polished metal. "The cursor is a light. It falls on things across the whole site" and Hero item 10 are softened to that for the hero.

You have my permission to go ahead and start building."
    - In exact terms:
    - **"No two pieces move alike" is struck.** The debris is one scene, held together: pieces near each other share a motion, a slow common drift or turn, with differences inside it. Variety serves the whole and is not a rule of its own.
    - **The test for the debris, and for the world:** would this pass in a premium film, or in a AAA game. If not, it is not good enough.

Claude Code builds the finished world and everything that moves, after he signs the look off.
- **Picture 9 is the target, whole (Nov 15, 20:10).** He looked at the stills of the fourth round while it was being built and found the layout of the city, the dunes, the trees and everything else different from picture 9. His words: "It should look exactly like this with just the couple of changes we just agreed on." So picture 9 (`reference/concept/09-*`) is the layout of the whole world, the top as well as the underside:
    - How it is seen: nearly level, about 5 degrees above, so the top is a thin strip and the city reads as a skyline. The fourth round was seen from 12 degrees, which showed a wide park and a wide field of dunes.
    - The planet's proportions: wider and shallower, about 2.75 wide to 1 deep. The fourth round's was about 2 to 1.
    - The city: dense, around 90 buildings, tall and low packed together, across the left four fifths of the top, with the spire and its ring at the middle, a tall gold pointed tower left of it, a stepped gold crown and the gold sun disc right of it, and gold caps further right.
    - The pyramid: large, at the front left edge of the land, a corner toward the visitor, bright polished gold.
    - The trees: a narrow belt along the front edge of the city. No park and no avenue.
    - The dunes: the far right of the top only, low, running to the planet's right rim.
    - The hollow: about two thirds of the planet's width, under a thick jagged roof, with a floor under the core that the stream and the small streams pour from.
    - **This replaces the entries of 17:46 and 18:43 above where they made picture 4 the layout of the top** (about 40 large towers standing apart, its trees in rows, its avenue to the front edge, its large dunes). Fable had built the fourth round's top from picture 4 and kept its own planet shape and view. That was Fable's reading of 17:46 and 18:43, and it was wrong.
    - Picture 4 stays a reference for one thing: how good its dunes are, with deep hollows in full shadow, which he asked for at 18:43.
    - The changes from picture 9 that stand: the dunes in picture 9's place and size but as good as picture 4's, the debris made new (much more of it, close under the planet, floating, one scene), the hero's own moon as the one fixed light, and stills only until he signs off. **His yes (Nov 15, 20:15): those four are the only changes from picture 9.** Everything else follows it.
- **The fourth round as rebuilt to picture 9 (Nov 15, 20:55).** At `/lab/hero-blockout`. Stills in `reference/studies/65-*`. Not yet seen by him.
    - What follows picture 9: the view (6 degrees), the planet's outline with its bottom torn open, the dense skyline (118 buildings, with the gold pointed tower left of the spire, the stepped gold crown and the gold sun disc right of it, gold caps further right, six gold roofs), the gold pyramid at the front left with a corner toward the visitor, the belt of trees along the city's front, the dunes at the right end, the thick roof over a hollow about two thirds of the planet's width, the core hanging in it with the stream pouring from under it, five small streams, the cracks lit from inside on the dark left side, the cratered moonlit right side.
    - The four agreed changes: the dunes have a sharp crest and a hollow face in full shadow, the debris is new (11 great pieces, two of them carrying a piece of the city, three arms of middling pieces round the stream, clumps lower down, dust), the moon is one fixed light from the upper right, nothing moves.
    - Where Fable finds it at the bar: how close the whole is to picture 9, the hollow's light and shadow, the cracks, the craters, the dunes, the pyramid's gold.
    - Where Fable finds it short, plainly: buildings seen close are simple forms with windows on a grid, loose pieces seen close are faceted solids with a rough face and not film rock, the city is greyer than picture 9's golden one, the core's grain is faint, the cracks are finer than picture 9's, the small streams are plain lines. The night sky and the moon itself are the page's and are not in this study.
    - What his sign off settles: the layout and the look to build to. What it does not: the finish of buildings and loose rock, which Claude Code's build takes further on his graphics card, and everything that moves.
- **His verdict on the world rebuilt to picture 9, and what the build must be (Nov 15, 20:47).** "I think this is a good starting point, but honestly, it's still a far cry from the quality that Gemini gave us. I just want to make sure that Claude Code is not gonna rebuild this as is with minor updates. It needs to look tremendously better. It needs to be aware of the quality level." In exact terms:
    - The study at `/lab/hero-blockout` is the layout and a starting point. It is not the look, and it is not the floor to beat by a little.
    - The quality to reach is picture 9's. The build is not a port of the study: its meshes and shaders are not carried over with small changes.
    - Whatever is handed to Claude Code has to state that quality level so it cannot be missed.
    - He has not yet said yes to the layout in so many words. Fable has asked for that yes.
    - Still standing: item 12 of the target above, made in code and not in Blender.
- **The hero's world, the build (agreed Nov 15, 20:52).** His answer to Fable's proposal: "Sounds great to me. Go ahead and update the documentation however you need to and give me the prompt to hand over to Claude code." What he agreed to:
    1. **The layout is approved, and only the layout:** where everything is, how big it is and how it is seen, as the study at `/lab/hero-blockout` has it. Changing any of that is asked of him first.
    2. **The look is not approved.** The quality to reach is picture 9's. The build does not carry the study's meshes or shaders over with small changes.
    3. **The bar is in the repo:** `QUALITY.md`, "The hero's world, built to picture 9", and the kit, `reference/kits/world.md`, with a picture for each part in `reference/concept/09-parts/`.
    4. **How it is built:** in code, not in Blender. Each part by its own Opus builder with Fable judging side by side, as the desk was.
    5. **A slice first:** the hollow with the core, the pyramid, and one tower (the stepped gold crown), each to full finish, shown to him beside picture 9 before anything else is built. If it is not tremendously better than the study, the method changes.
    6. Not ruled, and kept out of the slice: the two loose pieces that carry a piece of the city, and the line of light under gold and bronze crowns, which are Fable's and not in picture 9.
    7. Still open from before: whether the first screen ends at the same place of the world on every screen, the hero's opening, and when the new world goes onto `/lab/hero`.
- **Picture 9 is recreated, not our version of it (Nov 15, 21:38).** He saw the first output of the build in Claude Code: a smooth cut planet with a box for a hollow, grey stand ins on top. His words: "It looks much more like our design versus what Gemini provided. I think what Gemini provided looks better, and I'd rather try to recreate that perfectly than our inferior version. Maybe what makes sense is to have him keep referencing the image to make sure everything matches perfectly." In exact terms:
    - Picture 9 is the shape as well as the look: its outline, its proportions, where each thing is and how big, and how the rock is broken.
    - Where the study at `/lab/hero-blockout` and picture 9 differ, picture 9 wins, without asking him. The study is a rough guide to units, names and rough sizes.
    - **This replaces item 1 of "The hero's world, the build" above (20:52), which approved the study's layout and had any change to it asked of him first.** Fable wrote that rule, and it is why the build followed the study's smooth description of the hollow.
    - Every round of the build is laid over picture 9 and checked before anything else is judged (`reference/kits/world.md`, "Match picture 9, every round", and test 11 in `QUALITY.md`).
    - Unchanged: the four changes from picture 9 he agreed at 20:15, made in code and not in Blender, and the slice first.
- **The layout is picture 9's exactly, and the look is to be better than the picture's (Nov 15, late, to Claude Code).** Asked whether the goal was to recreate the picture perfectly or to make a better version of it in the same layout: "I apologize for the confusion, Fable. I just wanted you to preserve the exact layout of the Google Gemini reference image. Building placements, how tall the buildings are, and the overall looking feel should be improved upon. be much higher quality. You must look at the quality documents and make discernment yourself on what would be a visually better asset." And: "Currently we know what we want it to look like. Once we have that built, we need to figure out how can we make it visually more appealing for the user to look at it. Like these objects are real in 3D space, not real in the sense of something you would believe you would see in the real world, but whatever they're looking at looks like a real object lighting and shadows used to the advantage and so on. I don't forget the core has to tie in with our stream that we currently have. It has to look seamless, like the stream exists there and come from it." In exact terms:
    - **The layout is the picture's, exactly:** where each thing is, how big it is, how tall each building is, the planet's outline, where the hollow, the core and the pyramid are. The overlay (`npm run match`) is how that is held, every round.
    - **The look and feel is not a copy of the picture's rendering.** It is to be a much higher quality asset than the picture, judged by `QUALITY.md` and by Fable's own discernment of what is visually better. This softens "Picture 9 is the shape as well as the look" of 21:38 above: the shape stands, and the picture's look is the starting point and the least it may be, not the limit.
    - **The order:** first build what we already know it should look like. Then work out how to make it more appealing to look at.
    - **What "real" means here:** not something a person would believe exists in the real world, but whatever they are looking at reads as a real object in 3D space, with light and shadow used to its advantage.
    - **The core ties in with the stream the site already has.** It has to look seamless, as if the stream exists there and comes from the core.
- **Keep working until the world is fully finished, on a page that scrolls from the hero to Contact (Nov 15, late, to Claude Code, before he went to sleep).** "Great, I like your recommendations, but I am actually going to sleep. I say just keep working until this asset is fully finished, the whole site should be scrollable from the hero all the way down to the contact section." In exact terms:
    - **This replaces "stop after the slice".** The whole world is built, not three parts of it: the rock and the skin, the core and the streams, the land and its dunes, the pyramid, the spire, every tower, the trees, and what broke away.

That's why I interjected earlier, because you were trying to make it look more like the one on the left, which looks nowhere near as good as the one on the right." In exact terms:
    - The built tower of round 1 (pale stone piers with their courses, the bay of windows, the fluted stepped gold crown with its cornices and point) is what he wants, over the picture's small plain one. It is kept, and was put back exactly after a second round had begun changing it toward the picture.

        - **Now:** two flat pale grey piers with fine courses and no relief, a flat dark bay between them with small square lit windows scattered on a grid, and the gold stepped crown on top. Beside the city as it has become (buildings with two lit faces, setbacks, glass and stone, rooms behind their windows) it is the flattest and the coldest thing on the skyline.
        - **To be:** brought up to the rest, and past it, since it is one of the skyline's named towers. Fable's reading of how, his to turn: it keeps its place, its footprint, its height and the stepped crown's outline (those are picture 9's layout), and it keeps what he liked in it (pale stone piers, a bay of windows between them, a fluted stepped gold crown with its point). The stone is warmed into the city's light, the piers get relief and step in toward the crown, the bay gets depth and rooms, and the crown is lit with a direction.
        - **What this lifts:** "It is kept" above. The tower may now be changed. The second sentence stands: it is never made plainer.
        - **Built (Nov 16, about 08:40)** and on the live site: warm cream piers with relief that step in at three shoulders with gold caps to meet the crown, a deep bay of floors lit in runs under a gold sunburst, the crown as it was on a reeded frieze. `reference/studies/68-*` is before and after. Waiting on his look, and on his word on the stone: warm cream as built, or pale grey as it was.
    - Fable had told that round's builder to copy the picture's plain crown, narrow piers and fine glitter. That was Fable's misreading of "recreate picture 9" and is withdrawn.
- **The debris field of the original plan is kept (Nov 15, late, to Claude Code).** "In the original plan there was a debris field attached to it that was sent over to you, and then we pivoted to focusing more on the way the photo looked. And now we have a new way of doing it, but I want to make sure that debris field wasn't lost. We do in fact need a debris field under this object." In exact terms:
    - The field under the planet is the one the study planned and he was shown (`reference/studies/65-*-what-broke-away.jpg`): its great plates, the arms of middling pieces round the stream, the clumps lower down, the chips and the dust, each kind with its way of moving (plate, whirl, drift, tumble) and its family. It is copied out of the study as numbers (`scripts/world/debris-plan.json`, 1,397 pieces) and built from them.
    - A piece is moved or left out only where the new world needs it (the planet is shallower and its core higher than when the field was planned), and the two great boulders go where picture 9 has them.

- **His flags on a working still: the city looks less dense, and the dunes are to be sharper (Nov 15, 22:58, to Claude Code).** With picture 9, a working still of the hero (plain stand ins for the buildings, trees and land), and a close picture of dunes from an earlier iteration: "I just want a flag that it doesn't look as dense. Are we missing buildings and or trees? From the layout? One more thing I do want to note is I want the sand dunes to be sharper. Take a look at this image from my earlier iteration. To me, it looks much better than the current iteration." In exact terms:
    - **Density is checked against picture 9.** What Fable found: no building was missing (118 planned, 117 on the land). 90 of 285 trees were: they had been planted along the study's land edge, and lost their ground when the edge was corrected to the picture's deeper bite. The belt is planted again along the picture's edge (`scripts/world/placed.mjs`), 260 trees. The still looked sparse mostly because the buildings in it were unlit boxes. Density is judged again, laid over the picture, once the city and the trees are built.
    - **The dunes are sharper: a ruling.** Crescent dunes with knife sharp crests and a steep hollow face in deep shadow, as in his picture (kept for the builder as `shots/world/ref/dunes-he-likes.png`, not committed: it is a screenshot from his iPad), in picture 9's place. The soft rounded mounds of the plan's dune field are not what he wants. This is the first of the four agreed changes, said again more strongly.
- **The core, the stream and what broke away are one scene, and one story (Nov 15, late, to Claude Code).** "The debris field is super important. We have the core, which is the source of the stream that goes through the site. The stream has particles in it that are sharp and jagged. It's meant to be implied that these jagged particles are pieces of the exploded planet. So we'll need to tie the core in with the stream, so it looks like a legitimate source for the stream, also, doubling as the planet's core, the stream essentially is just a beam of energy being shot out of core so the stream has to perfectly tie into it, and the debris field decor, and the wreckage under the planet all have to cohesively look like they belong. And they're part of the same scene." In exact terms:
    - **The core is two things at once:** the planet's core, and the source of the stream that runs down the whole site.
    - **The stream is a beam of energy shot out of the core.** It leaves the core with force. It is not a drip and not a fall. Where it leaves, it is already the stream: the join is perfect.
    - **The stream's sharp jagged shards are pieces of the exploded planet.** So the smallest of what broke away and the shards the stream carries are the same stuff, and a visitor should be able to see pieces of the planet being caught by the beam and carried down the site.
    - **The debris field, the wreckage under the planet, the core and the stream belong to one scene:** the same rock, the same light, each lit by the others. The beam's light lies on the wreckage and on the pieces near it.
    - The debris field is "super important": it is not decoration to be thinned or dropped.
- **A standing permission for the night of Nov 15 (to Claude Code, as he signed off).** Fable asked for it, naming its limit, and he answered: "Yes, I agree to all the standing permissions that you have requested, and whatever else you need to make this happen. I'm signing off. I'm excited to see what you do overnight, partner!" In exact terms:
    - Where something is within the written rules and is good but not amazing, Fable may go past a rule tonight where that clearly looks better. The version that keeps the rule stays one switch away, and every such call is listed for him in the morning. He then keeps or reverses each: none of them is a new rule until he says so.
    - The three rules Fable named as the limit: **"Gold is at the palette's hue, never yellow"** (the world's gold may go a few degrees toward true gold, checked beside his portrait, never as far as the second build's yellow). **"Nothing blooms at rest"** (a tight glow on lit windows and gold glints, never round the core). **"The view never moves"** (a very small shift of the view, a degree or two, so things slide past one another).
    - "Whatever else you need to make this happen" is read narrowly by Fable: what the world needs in order to be finished and on the page. It is not leave to change other sections or anything else he has approved.
- **Keep refining, part by part, and do not stop (Nov 16, in the night, to Claude Code).** "I should've specified, I do want you to make enhancements on that pass section by section. Keep refining. I just realized that Fable 5.2 won't be available on the max plan, so I essentially just want you to keep working and refining the site until I lose access. Don't stop working, no matter what! If you finish all that, go ahead and do anything else you need to get the site ready for deployment." In exact terms:
    - **The pass that makes the world better than the picture is wanted now, part by part,** not held until the layout is whole: each part is taken past the picture's look as it is built, and refined again.
    - **Fable keeps working until he loses access to it.** No stopping to wait for him, and no stopping when the world is "done": there is always a next round.
    - **When the world is as good as Fable can get it, the rest of the site is made ready to deploy.** Fable's reading, his to correct: that means what a deployment needs (the build, memory on his iPad, speed, errors, the page scrolling end to end, the record true), not changes to sections he has approved.
    - **What follows from "until I lose access", Fable's own reading:** the work can end at any moment, so the repo is kept ready to be picked up by someone else at every step: each round committed and pushed, `LOG.md` and `PLAN.md` true, and what is short of the bar written down plainly.
- **After the hero: a pass over the whole site, then phones and the iPad, and a preloader (Nov 16, in the night, to Claude Code).** "Once you finish with the hero scene, do one more pass and go through the whole entire website section by section. figure out where you can make improvements on the 3D assets, overall user experience, animations, user interface, micro interactions, then, after you completed all that, let's start fine tuning the experience for mobile and iPad users. Also ensure that the site is performant, create a preloader, letting users know upfront that we're loading the website." In exact terms, in his order:
    1. **Finish the hero's world.**
    2. **One more pass, section by section, over the whole site** (the hero, Work, Skills, About, Contact, the footer): find where each can be better and make it better, in five things he named: the 3D assets, the experience of using it, the animations, the interface, and the small interactions (hover, press, focus, a thing answering the pointer).
    3. **Then phones and the iPad.** This is the mobile pass that "How we work" holds until every section is finished: he has now called it, to begin when item 2 is done.
    4. **Throughout: the site is fast**, measured, not assumed.
    5. **A preloader:** the first thing a visitor sees says plainly that the site is loading, before anything heavy is shown.
    - **Fable's reading of how far "make improvements" goes, his to correct:** it is leave to improve what is there, not to overturn what he has ruled. Where a better version would go against something in Settled, Fable does not build over it: it is put to him as a recommendation, in bold, naming the rule, as `CLAUDE.md` asks. His copy stays his. Every change made in the pass is listed for him, section by section, with what it was and what it is now.
- **Calls Fable made in the pass and for the live site, the night of Nov 16. None is a rule, and each is his to keep or reverse** (what each was and what it is now is in `LOG.md`, top entry, and `reference/pass/findings.md` is the list they came from):
    1. **Skills:** the lists are a label column and a hanging block of items, a little larger, with no line beginning or ending on a dot.
    2. **About:** a change of tab crosses the line under the tabs and fades the new words in. Thrown from the studio light, the stream's end no longer rests on his face: once out of the lens it goes on in behind him.
    3. **Work:** the stream's end no longer rests just short of the bar, a card or a stone (`STREAM.work.snap`, and 0 puts it back). This touches "a speed no faster than the scroll" (Nov 12) by at most 22 units, as Skills and Contact already did. View all work goes to his live site's projects until he says where it goes.
    4. **Contact:** the form says what is wrong with a field in the plate's own colors, a message that did not send says so where his city stands, the confirmation rises in and takes focus, Send message is a metal button, and the wells are a dark of the plate's bronze. The words a field says are stand ins. The résumé link is held back until he says where the file lives.
    5. **The hero:** the scroll cue has a halo of the page's ground and a small tick and stays as still text with reduced motion. On an iPad the world is drawn with more pixels (`HANDHELD.world`), which costs about 100 MB at the foot of the hero.
    6. **Everywhere:** a 3D piece made while it is on the screen comes on over a quarter second. Never the first piece of a page that has not been scrolled: there is still no opening.
    7. **The desk:** only the keyboard's cut edge is changed, from a cold pale line to a warm satin. Two rounds that tried to relight the desk made it worse and none of that is kept.
    8. **The small pieces:** the reflector's tube and the studio light's drum and yoke each carry a thin line of light that follows the pointer. The reflector's answers wherever the pointer is. The studio light's drops out with the pointer low and left, and a round that mended that lost it with the pointer at the right, so it is as it was. Nothing else about the three is changed: the CPU is as it was to the byte. One soft blown spot on the studio light's bezel is capped.

    10. **The hero's rock:** under the hollow's floor there is to be dim rock, the far inner wall of the planet, with the stream falling in front of it, as picture 9 has it. Ours is black air there until the rock's seventh round lands.
    11. **The hero's city:** every building is turned about 22 degrees more so its right face shows. The named tops do not move. His stepped tower was left exactly as he approved it until he released it on Nov 16 (above, "Released").
- **His look at the whole page on his Mac (Nov 16, about 09:20, to Claude Code). Rulings and wishes, for the whole site and not only the hero.** His words: "The hero section looks amazing. It is a tad bit low frame rates, though. I wonder if we can fix the frame rate issue without compromising any of the quality. The hero's also a bit more static than I would like, maybe we can have more stuff going on in the sky? To make it feel more alive. The debris field looks incredible." In exact terms, each as he said it:

I would prefer if everything shared the same background." It continues down the site. Now: bands of slightly different ground from Work down, on his screen. To be: one ground everywhere. (Not yet seen in the test browser at three widths: a screenshot of it was asked of him.)

There's like a golden bar going across the bottom of the screen that shouldn't be there, a third one that spans the whole one like a border, that needs to be removed." It was the rail's own plain outline showing under the stream's rail on the whole page. Mended the same morning.

To be: the first screen shows the city at its foot, and the core comes with the scroll.

I feel like that would add a lot to the site, especially on the iPad portrait. I feel like it has the power and the space to really have a similar experience to the desktop." Now: on a narrow screen the stream runs down Work's two sides and stops. To be, at the least: it strikes Work's cards on the way down, and comes back at the foot of the page to launch the rocket. On an upright iPad: as near the desktop's whole experience as fits. This lifts Open 32's "nothing of it runs on over Skills, About and Contact".
        - **Built (Nov 16, about 15:00), not seen by him, at its least as he put it.** Under 1100 px: each side of the stream turns in to strike each card that is its own, once, on the card's upright side a little under its top, and the strike wakes the card by its band, as on a wide screen (a card that stands to one side is that side's, and in one column the cards are the left's and the right's in turn). A card the stream has not reached is asleep as the blank, where before no card slept on a narrow screen. Under Work's button nothing of the stream is seen through Skills and About. It comes on again beside Contact's title, falls beside the plate, turns in and lands on the stone, and the stone lights and the rocket launches, live, in place of the pad's picture. The rocket climbs in front of the plate and out of the top of the screen. The plate itself is still the plain painted one, awake from the start. **Not built: an upright iPad as near the desktop's whole experience as fits** (the live world, the live desk, the rows' things, About's route). That is still the mobile pass's to design. **What it costs:** in WebKit a phone holds 1122 MB of GPU at the page's foot where it held 817 with the pad as a picture, and an upright iPad 1152. That is over the line of about 1000 the brief sets from Work's page, and under what an iPad held sideways holds (2088). It needs his phone and his iPad to say whether it is safe.
    9. **The word Scroll goes from the hero. A ruling.** "I think we should remove the word scroll at the bottom."

Those buttons actually look really good, but I don't really want a button that moves people around the site on this particular portfolio. Instead maybe we can repurpose them. One of them could be download my resume, and I'm not sure what the other one should be. Maybe it takes you to my dedicated work page that we haven't built yet?" Now: See the work and Get in touch, which scroll the page. To be: no button that moves a visitor down this page. One becomes the résumé. The other is open: his own thought is the dedicated work page, which is not built. This touches Hero item 2 (the buttons go to Work and Contact) and the résumé link held back for his phone number.
        - **Settled (Nov 16, about 09:55).** "I agree with your recommendation for the buttons. Can you actually just make a placeholder all work page and send them there when they click the button." So: the hero's buttons are View all work and Download résumé. View all work goes to a page of all his work on this site, `/work`, which is a plain placeholder until that page is designed (it links back home and to his current site). Work's own View all work button goes to the same page. Download résumé is in the code and held back (`hero.resume.held` in `src/content/hero.ts`) until he sends a copy without his phone number: until then the hero shows one button. Hero item 2 now reads: one button to the page of all his work and one for his résumé, and none that scrolls this page.
    12. **His résumé, the domain and the striped ground (about 10:20). Rulings.**
        - **The résumé is on the site as he supplied it, phone number and all.** "I've attached my résumé to the main route folder of this project." "I don't care about my phone number being on my résumé, just use it." The file is `public/downloads/BrandonChurch-Resume.pdf` (his `BrandonChurch-Resume-FDE.pdf`). The hero's Download résumé button and the footer's Résumé link are on. **This lifts "no phone number" for the résumé file only:** the site's own pages still show none (Contact, Settled).
        - **The domain is brandonchurchportfolio.com.** He typed "brandonchurchpoprtfolio.com", and his résumé prints brandonchurchportfolio.com: Fable took the résumé's spelling and told him so. He sets up DNS himself. `SITE_URL` in `src/config/site.ts` is that address.
        - **The striped ground is dropped.** "I cannot make that stripe appear again so we can disregard that." Point 4 above is closed unless it comes back.
    13. **The two new things in Skills, and the stripes again (about 10:50). Rulings.**
        - **The second row's thing is not a computer part.** "For the 3D object for full stack engineering I do not like the idea of having another computer component there. I think we should figure out other options." The screen with its back off is out of the page (its code is kept in `src/pieces/screen/`, set aside). This replaces his pick of Nov 14 for that row. What it becomes is open: Fable's options were put to him the same hour.
        - **The bar for all three is the CPU's.** "It also doesn't look as good as the AI product engineering 3D object. The quality bar for full stack and design should be just as high as that one, else it will look out of place."
        - **The palette and brush stays and is to be greatly improved.** "The current design systems one doesn't look great either. Not necessarily saying I want to toss it, but it needs to be greatly improved."
