Can you remove them please?" The ruling of point 4 stands: one ground for the whole page. Fable's finding: on a machine slow enough to be given the stream's middle or low tier, the stream's canvas was drawn at a part ratio of the screen's pixels (1.34 to 2, say) and stretched, and its grain beat against the screen as a faint regular pattern over the whole ground. Mended in `src/stream/stream.ts` (whole steps only). Not yet confirmed by his eye.
    - **Point 1, the hero's frame rate, mended at the first screen (Nov 16, about 16:30).** The top of the hero holds the screen's rate on his Mac (16.6 ms a frame where it took 28), with the picture unchanged: the land, which cost more than the rest of the world together and never changes, is drawn once and kept (`LOG.md`, Nov 16, "About 16:30"). By the core it is 21.6 ms where it was about 35, and not yet at the screen's rate: the rock and the debris are next.
    16. **A row's title is two lines, so its lists have the room (about 17:15). A ruling, built.** With a screenshot of the rows on his iPad: "I don't love how the sub categories are pushed so far to the side and all the tags are forced to stay in that little space. I think the titles are taking way too much width. I think we should probably line break all of them." Each title is now two lines (AI Product / Engineering, Full-Stack / Engineering, Design Systems / & Craft: the last word goes under the rest, and an "&" goes down with it), in a column 200 units wide where it had 271, and the lists have 632 units where they had 561. On a screen of one column a title stays on one line.
    14. **The second row's thing is a pocket watch, opened (about 11:05). A ruling.** "Let's go with the pocket watch, your recommendation." The dial is the front end and the movement behind it the back end, in the site's own antique metal, at the CPU's bar. Kit: `reference/kits/watch.md`. **In the page since about 17:20, its second round, not seen by him:** a gilt movement, a polished gold dial, the stream's light passing wheel by wheel. Fable's judgment: it reads at once as an opened pocket watch and sits with the other two, and it is one family of gold, with less contrast than the CPU has.
    15. **The stripes, shown (about 11:05).** With a screenshot (`shots/world/ref/stripe-his-screenshot-nov-16.png` on this machine): above Work's title, on the dev server, the stream's light on the ground ends at a level line a little over the title, the ground is darker above it, and the stream itself has a small cut at the same height. "It's unbelievably faint and hard to notice but it's there. This also happens again at the bottom of the work section", and at the bottom of About. His own reading: "the way the light ends abruptly per section instead of persisting through". "It might not even be a huge thing that we need to worry about fixing, what do you think?" Fable's answer: fix it, since a cut in the stream is the one thing on the page that must never show, and it is not found yet in the test browser.
    11. **The line across the foot of Work, again (about 09:55):** "That gold border I was referring to, it was gone now, not sure if you fixed it or if it was just a glitch." It was real and is mended (point 5).
- **The dunes are taller (Nov 16, in the night, to Claude Code).** "I think the sand dunes should be taller." Said on seeing the world on the page. In exact terms:
    - **Which:** the dunes of the desert at the right end of the top of the world.
    - **Now:** twelve crescent dunes whose crests stand 4 to 15 units over the sand (a low building beside them is 20 to 30, a tree about 15), so at the page's size they read as ripples on a flat.
    - **To be:** about twice that. The tallest crests about 30 units, most 16 to 24, the small ones between them 9 to 12, each steeper for it, with the sharp crest and the deep hollow face he already ruled. Their places and the desert's outline stay picture 9's.
    - This changes one thing the picture set (how tall the dunes are), by his word. Fable's reading of how much is Fable's: he gave no number, and it is his to turn up or down.
- **The dunes again: a lot taller, and their shadows are wrong (Nov 16, about 02:00, to Claude Code).** On the live site, after the first taller round: "The dunes are still not good enough, the shadows aren't hitting it right to make it look realistic and they need to be a lot taller." In exact terms:
    - **A lot taller.** "About double" was not enough. The tallest dunes now stand about 60 units, as tall as a mid sized building beside them, most 35 to 50, the small ones between them 15 to 25. Each is a hill a third to a half as tall as it is wide.
    - **The shadows.** What he saw: a pale flat plain with dark crescents let into it. No dune threw a shadow on anything, the dark of a slip face was one flat value with a hard outline all round, and the lit sand was one value everywhere, so nothing said which way the light came. What is wanted: one light plainly from one side (the hero's own moon, behind the world and to the visitor's right, low). Every dune lit on the side toward it and shaded on the other, its lit slope brightest where it faces the moon and falling off as it turns away. A long shadow cast from each crest across the sand to its left, climbing the next dune. Shade that is deep but not dead: warmer and lighter where the lit sand beside it bounces light in.
    - Their places and the desert's outline stay picture 9's.

- An idea to revisit with the hero, not decided: his first thought was the city breaking apart into the stream as the visitor scrolls. Fable's twist, which he asked to have noted: building, not breaking. The city assembles out of the stream's facets in the opening, and the stream carries the same pieces on down the page. (Nov 12)

**The hero, the city at rest: the agreement (Nov 14)**

For Claude Code, added to the work it has in hand. His words are in the list above. **Changed the same night: the city no longer stands on a flat disc. It stands on the top of a small planet that has been torn in half.** Items 1, 3, 4, 7, 8 and 12 are rewritten for that and items 14 to 18 are new. The wireframe at `/lab/hero-draft` (`src/hero/plan.ts`) is redrawn and is the basic version: item 18 says what it settles. The opening is not part of this.

1. **One page, `/lab/hero`,** laid out as the wireframe has it: his words centered about a third of the way down, and the world under them on the center line, the tip of its spire a little under the buttons.
2. **The words block:** the line above his name, his name, his first paragraph, and the real primary and secondary metal buttons, one to Work and one to Contact. The words are his, as `HeroDraft.tsx` has them. The buttons' words are stand ins.
3. **At rest only the top of the world shows.** On every desktop screen: the spire and the crown of the dome with the city's core on it, curving away to both sides and down under the foot of the screen. A taller screen shows more of it. The torn edge and everything under it, the planet's inside, its core and the debris, are under the foot of the screen until the page is scrolled. Nothing of the world is ever behind the words.
4. **The world is made again, as a piece.** The top of a small planet: a dome a little less than half a sphere, curved side up, about 1320 units across at its torn edge, with a spire about 220 tall on its top (`PLANET` in `src/hero/plan.ts`). It is art, so what stands on it is far larger than it would be on a planet. A city of the future as Star Trek draws one, with the flair of ancient Egypt: the spire at the top with rings round it, as the carried city's has, a core of towers round it, and down the world's sides the other places a small world would have: gardens, terraces, farms, monuments, open ground, craft in the air, whatever the builder chooses. It is not towers from edge to edge. Its nature is in the site's own colors and not green. Gold, bronze and copper are used freely on it, polished, so it belongs with the rest of the site. Of "The look" in `reference/kits/README.md` it keeps this: it is never one orange mass, and its materials can be told apart. The second build's model is a reference and is not the model used. **It is to be the best 3D thing on the site.** Items 19 to 27 say what the finished version of it has.
5. **It is alive.** Lit windows and small lights in the palette's colors, and whatever small movement makes a far off city feel lived in, cheap enough to draw every frame.
6. **Its glow.** A soft glow behind and above it, of the kind behind his portrait in About: the light a far off city throws into the sky. It has no edge and no rectangle.
7. **Streams of the stream's own light leave the torn face** at several places, and the main one leaves the core. Nothing the second build drew. There is no rim now, and no falls off one.
8. **The real stream comes out of the core.** It sets off once the core's underside has been scrolled up to the reading line, falls through the debris, and runs to Work's bar, which it strikes dead center.
9. **A night sky, of another world.** Stars at the top, in the corners and down the sides, none behind the words, pale and warm with no blue, fewer and fainter toward the world's glow, and gone before Work begins. Most are fine points of differing size and brightness. The brightest few are stars with four points of light. It looks real and finely detailed, not a scatter of dots. Also in it, for the builder to make and show him: a moon of that world in an upper corner, a faint band of far stars across the other upper corner, and now and then a meteor. None of these is behind the words or brighter than the world.
10. **The cursor's light falls on the world,** as on everything else.
11. **No opening in this pass.** The world and the words are simply there when the page loads. The opening is designed on the finished world, with him. Nothing draws its outline.
12. **It goes on top of the whole page** (`/lab/whole`), with the stream unbroken from the core into Work. **Work stands further under it than before:** about 480 units under the lowest of the debris, so that the torn underside, the core and the debris can fill a screen with no part of Work in sight.
13. With reduced motion nothing moves. Under 1100 px the page is a plain stand in.
14. **The underside is torn, not cut.** It looks as if the planet had been ripped in half: broken and jagged, different all the way round, hanging lowest round the core. No flat base, no clean plane, no keel made for a fall. Where it is torn the planet's inside shows in section: its layers, crust over deeper rock, with veins of bright metal in it. Near the edge the world above is damaged too: ground that is cracked, something leaning or fallen.
15. **The core.** At the planet's middle, a little under the level of the torn edge, about 300 units across. From the page's view it is plainly seen through the tear: how the tear is shaped so that it is, is the builder's. It is a 3D thing that burns and moves, not a flat disc and not a ball with a glow laid over it. It is the stream's source, so it is the stream's own light: the same colors, the same kind of core of light, with the stream's shards about it. It is not lava. It lights the torn rock round it and the debris. It costs nothing while it is off the screen.
16. **Debris.** Round the torn underside and under it: pieces of the planet, from slabs with a length of street or a building still on them down to shards the size of the stream's. Some in slow orbit round the world, some holding their place with one face to the core, some tumbling. Many kinds and sizes and ways of moving, so it reads as a world that really was broken in half. The smallest are the stream's own shards, so the debris and the stream are one family, and the stream falls through it. It thins out toward Work. None of it is ever over the words.
17. **It looks like a real thing that was photographed.** "I want the user to look like they're looking at a real thing, so it has to look like a hyper realistic object, even though that may not be a realistic scene you would see in the universe."
18. **What the wireframe settles:** the world's sizes, how much of it shows at rest, where the core is, where the stream sets off, and how much room there is before Work. **What it does not:** the world's look, the shape of the tear, what stands where on it, how many pieces of debris there are and how each moves.

*The finished version of what stands on the world, agreed at midnight on Nov 14 from his look at the first build (`reference/studies/58-hero-the-world-as-first-built-whole.jpg`). The planet under it is right and is left alone.*

19. **The city pulls back from the edge.** As dense as it is now. It ends about a quarter of the way in from the edge all the way round, which is about 58 of the dome's 78 degrees from its top, and two or three wedges of open land cut further in toward the middle. As first built it covered the surface to its edge: "maybe it covers slightly too much going to the edge".
20. **Bare ground shows at the rim,** so the curve of the planet is seen, and the tear is ground breaking and not buildings ending.
21. **Polished metal, used boldly.** "It's missing that shiny metal look. I don't see any of it. We could do some really cool things with it. Remember this is art so we have some flexibility." Real gold, bronze and copper that reflect, and answer the cursor's light as it moves. Not orange trim on grey boxes. Ideas, the builder's to choose from and to better: a mirror polished cap on the pyramid. Gilded rings and a gilded crown on the spire. A few whole towers clad in polished bronze among the stone ones. Domes and roofs in copper. The veins of bright metal in the torn rock coming up through the surface. Where a world would have lakes, pools of mirror polished metal that reflect the moon and the glow.
22. **A jungle,** where the nature area is now, at the left by the pyramid. Dense and layered, and far out of scale on purpose: its trees as tall as the buildings beside them, so that at the page's size a visitor knows it for a jungle at a glance. "Remember this is art. They don't have to be proportionate. I want them to be big enough for the user to understand that's a jungle." In the site's colors and not green.
23. **A second natural area on the opposite side,** the right, that looks completely different from the jungle. The builder's choice. Ideas: golden dunes with a pool of polished metal among them. Terraced fields that follow the curve of the ground.
24. **A monument where the mountain was,** at the back right. Spectacular, and not another pyramid. The mountain is dropped. The builder's design, shown to him. Ideas: a great armillary of polished gold rings that turns slowly. A colossal Sun disc standing on its rim, in polished gold, with the carving of Work's Sun stones. A great obelisk with a mirror cap. No figure and no face unless it can be made at the bar: those are where code is weakest.
25. **The craft in the air.** The specks go. Either a few designed craft, each large enough to be known for a craft at the page's size, with thin lanes of small moving lights between the towers, or none.
26. **More kinds of building,** and fewer copies of one grey block. The two blank pale blocks at the front of the avenue are designed or removed.
27. **Left alone:** the planet's body, the torn underside, the core, the debris, the streams from the torn face, the sky, the moon, the glow, the spire's shape, the layout and his words. Nothing already built is thrown away without need, and nothing a builder has in hand is overwritten.

*Fable's calls, each his to overturn:*

- What stands on the world stands straight out from the ground under it, so the skyline fans a little. That is what makes a dome read as a planet and not as a hill.
- The view stays from the front and nearly level, and does not move as the page scrolls: the world scrolls with the page.
- A taller screen shows more of the world. Nothing is drawn larger for it.
- The core sits where the sphere's middle is.
- A small cue at the foot of the first screen says there is more below, and goes once the page is scrolled.

*Not built in this pass:* the opening, the navigation, phones.

**The hero, the city at rest, as built (Nov 14, night). Study 30, at `/lab/hero`, and on top of `/lab/whole`. The city stands in it, as a first full build. The sky, the glow as the sky draws it, and the falls off the rim are not in yet. Nothing of it has been judged by an independent reviewer.**

- **Built by Fable:** the page (`HeroLab.tsx`, `src/hero/board.ts`), the stream's route from the main fall (`src/stream/route-hero.ts`, `src/hero/stage.ts`), the city's frame and its plan of lots (`src/pieces/skycity/index.ts`, `layout.ts`, `part.ts`), and the join that puts the hero in front of Work on the whole page.
- **Built by Opus builders, from `reference/kits/skycity.md`:** the ground (the walled rim with six made places for the falls, layered rock with ribs let into it, a keel with a mouth for the main fall, a floor of lit streets and parks), the spire (stone buttresses that become steel ribs, a glass core, five ringed decks, a lantern crown, a needle with a beacon and a lift that climbs), and the towers (fourteen kinds of building and four landmarks on 315 lots, windows lit as rooms and floors, beacons, about 300 cars and 26 craft from the clock).
- **What the builders' own fresh reviewers said, of all three parts:** at the page's size every one called it a floating city of the future. Every one also called it "a game or CG render, not a photographed model", and close up each part the weaker work beside his PC Builder's parts. The towers read to one as "a 1930s to 1980s American downtown". So test 2's first half passes and its second half does not.
- **The layout** is the wireframe's, worked out by the stylesheet from the screen's height: his words a third of the way down, the rim of the city's ground 40 units under the foot of the screen. Not yet measured on the four screens of test 1.
- **The stream** begins on the front of the rim under the spire and is not drawn until that place has been scrolled up to the reading line. It runs straight down the front of the rock to the keel, then falls swaying. On `/lab/hero` it strikes a stand in for Work's bar. On the whole page Work's own route takes it from 170 units over Work's title, so one stream runs from the main fall to the stone in Contact.
- **Not in yet:** the night sky, with the glow drawn by it (a builder has both: the page shows a plain glow meanwhile). The six falls off the rim and the main fall as standing light before the stream sets off. The cursor's cue is in. No opening, as agreed.
- **His look at the first full build (Nov 14, night): three changes, written into item 4.** Rings round the spire as the old city's has. Gold, bronze and copper used freely, polished. And not towers from rim to rim: a core of towers with other kinds of place round it, nature in the site's colors, "a Star Trek future city with ancient Egypt flair".
    - **The plan is redrawn** (`src/pieces/skycity/layout.ts`): towers only in the core, out to the second boulevard, and a low town behind it. Round the core, six places (`DISTRICTS`): the way to the spire on the page's center line, hanging gardens at the front right, a port at the right of the skyline, wild ground behind, a temple quarter at the left of the skyline, and terraced fields at the front left. About 150 lots where there were 315.
    - **Six builders are on it, one a part:** the spire again (rings, metals), the towers again (fewer, each designed, a future with Egypt in it and not a downtown), the ground again (a floor that follows the new plan, a rim in the metals, rock that is rock), and three new parts: the temple quarter with the way to the spire, the gardens with the farms and all the city's nature, and the port with every craft in the air. `reference/kits/skycity.md`, "Changed Nov 14, night".
    - **Fable's calls in the plan, his to overturn:** which places there are and where each lies. The temple quarter is where the Egypt in it is strongest and holds the left of the skyline with one great thing, a pyramid cased in polished metal or something as strong. The port holds the right. Gold is a polished metal at the palette's own hue, between `copper-light` and `hot`, and never a yellow brass.
    - **The live site still shows the first full build of the city** until the new parts are in: the plan with half its places empty is not pushed.
- **The falls are in (Nov 14, night), not yet pushed.** The main fall stands as the stream's own light from the rim down the rock, with the stream's leading end waiting at the keel until the scroll takes it on. Six more leave the rim at the plan's places and fall free, drawn by the stream's renderer as runs of its own core with nothing riding them (`falls` in `Planned`).
- **Fable's calls, his to overturn:** the hero's buttons are the real metal buttons with the stand in words. Work stands 330 units under the keel on the whole page. The towers' builder added a faint moonlight from the upper right, where the moon will be, so every tower has a lit side.

**The torn world, as built (Nov 14, late). It stands, at `/lab/hero` and on top of `/lab/whole`, and is on the live site as a first standing build after his second look: every part is real and none is at the bar. What follows was written when only the frame stood.**

- **Standing, by its builders:** the ground as land with a broken margin, a lake, a river and a fall. A dense city of 213 buildings in one battered family. A spire with stacked rings and a lit globe. The pyramid and its quarter at the jungle's edge, and the way to the spire as stepped terraces to a great gate. A jungle with giants. A port as a stepped massif, with traffic in the air. The torn underside in layers. The core as the stream's own line coiled into a knot that drains into the stream. Debris with pieces of the city on its slabs.
- **What each part's own fresh reviewers last said, and what Fable sees:** the city "a procedural city test render", its front rows "Monopoly houses". The ground "a built platform" in places and its mountains "draped cloth". The jungle "cheap, procedural" close up. The core "above stock, below AAA". The gate at the end of the way is two plain pale blocks. A band of blocks under the torn edge still reads as a rim. None of the reviewers has called any part a film or a AAA frame.
- **Measured:** in WebKit at an iPad's size it runs with no errors, on `/lab/hero` and `/lab/whole`. In the test browser it holds 60 frames a second, with the world's canvas held to 2.8 million pixels (`PIXELS` in `index.ts`): at a screen's full density it drew 30. Not yet measured on a 120 Hz screen or with the processor slowed. The first frame takes about 1.5 seconds and the ground about 1.3 seconds to build, in slices: far over what the first visit can bear.
- **Fable's own, since the frame:** the world's light is night (the moon as a raking key from the visitor's right, low fill, the cursor's light as an accent, fog toward darkness, the core lighting the torn rock), the torn edge bites up to 150 into the outline, and the mountains are one range.


- **Built by Fable:** the world's frame (`src/pieces/skycity/layout.ts`, `index.ts`, `part.ts`), the page's layout for it, the stream's route out of the core, the six streams from the torn face, and the join into Work.
- **The sizes began as the wireframe's** and were changed by his look at the frame, below: about 1320 across at the torn edge and 400 tall, a spire 240 tall, a core 300 across, 1105 from the spire's tip to the lowest of what broke away, and Work 480 under that.
- **Nine parts, one builder each:** the ground of the dome, the spire, the towers, the temple quarter, the gardens and farms, the port, the torn underside, the core, and what broke away. The first six carry over what was built for the disc. The disc and everything built for it is kept on the branch `kept/city-on-the-disc`.
- **The night sky is in** (`src/hero/sky`, by a builder): stars, ten with four points, a moon in the upper right, a band of far stars in the upper left, a meteor now and then, and the glow behind the world. It ends at the world's torn edge. Its own reviewers call it a night sky and a render, not a photograph, and not plainly another world's.
- **Fable's calls, each his to overturn:**
    - What stands on the world is planned on a flat map laid over the dome, so the places he has seen (the way to the spire, the gardens, the farms, the temple quarter, the port) are where they were, and each now reaches further out.
    - The moon is the world's key light, from the upper right where the moon is in the sky, with real shadows.
    - Only the front half of the world is ever seen, so nothing is spent on the far side.
    - From 760 on the map out to the torn edge the top of the world is broken: cracked, split, with things leaning and fallen.
    - The torn rock is torn back round the core into a hollow that is open toward the visitor, which is how the core is seen.
    - The six streams leave the tips of pieces of torn rock that hang toward the visitor.
    - The moon is alone: the sky's builder also made one with a ring and one with a second moon, and its reviewers found both weaker.

**His look at the frame (Nov 14, late), from the stills in the chat. Six rulings.** He saw the frame while the old disc was still standing on the new dome.

- "There should be no platform anymore. The top of the sphere is the planet where the city should reside." **No platform and no rim: the dome's own surface is the ground.**
- "The city also looks similar, it's what you had before, like you added this onto the existing design... the old design won't work here. This is a three-dimensional design. We have an entire sphere to cover." **The world is designed for the sphere, not moved onto it.**
- "The sphere is a bit rounder than I would've hoped." **The dome is flatter:** still about 1320 across, about 400 tall where it was 535 (`PLANET` in `src/pieces/skycity/layout.ts`: curved to a radius of 748, kept to 62 degrees). This replaces the sizes in item 4 and in the wireframe. The core keeps its place under the torn edge, which is no longer the sphere's own middle.
- "I like that you added a pyramid, but overall the ground looks way more like a space station, and the ring and everything to the right look really bad." **The pyramid stays. The ground is land,** hills, rock and soil, and nothing on it reads as a space station. **The port's ring and what stood with it are rejected,** and that side is designed again.
- "The city also looks really small. That should not be the case." **The city is large:** few, large buildings filling the crown, up to 185 tall, and the spire is 240.
- "I think the nature scenes should look much more like a national forest than a farm." **Nature is a wild forest,** as a national forest is. The farms and the terraced gardens are dropped. It is still in the site's colors and not green.
- **What "real" means, in his words (Nov 14, late):** "This is the most important one on the entire site, so it has to look the absolute best! Think Star Wars, alien landscape, but familiar. Ultra realistic in the sense of it looks like an actual real object the user is looking at, something from a high production movie or AAA video game. Not hyperrealistic in the sense of what you're looking at, you would actually see in the real world, as this is a futuristic sci-fi city." **So the bar for item 17 is the finish of a high production film or a AAA game: a thing with real weight, light and material. It is not that the scene could exist.** A reviewer who says "this looks like a film or a AAA game" has passed it. One who says cheap, amateur, low poly, a toy or a phone game has failed it. And the landscape is alien but familiar, as a Star Wars world is: landforms a visitor knows, made strange.
- **The core, in his words (Nov 14, late), on seeing the plain ball that stood in for it:** "It's very basic. Just an orange circle... we have to consider that the stream is coming out of that. So it should look like the culminating source of the stream, as well as a planet's core... this should tie in with the stream seamlessly." **So the core reads two ways at once:** the place all of the stream's light is gathered, at its most intense, and the heart of a planet. **And there is no place a visitor can point to where the core ends and the stream begins.** This sharpens item 15 and test 11. The join is built from both sides: the core by its builder, and how the stream begins by Fable.
- *Fable's calls in answering it, his to overturn:* the forest is in autumn color, which is the palette's own. The pyramid moves in toward the top so it shows at rest on every screen. The port stays a port, as the pyramid's match at the right of the skyline, with nothing on thin masts. The land rises up to 38 units in ridges outside the city. With the dome flatter the world is about 1105 from the spire's tip to the lowest of what broke away, where the wireframe has 1220.

**His second look (Nov 14, late), from working stills of the world mid-rebuild. Five rulings, which replace the plan made after his first look.** "It's coming along."

- "It looks like the city isn't dense enough. You think of a city from Star Trek or Star Wars, the city is ultra dense. Also don't like how spread out the buildings are." **The city is ultra dense:** buildings a few units apart, in layers one behind another, streets as canyons.
- "You can really see the grade of the planet, which I think we agreed to make it not such a rounded dome. It should be circular, and when you scroll down, you should have the realization that this is a piece of a planet, but it doesn't have to be so round." **The dome is a shallow cap:** still about 1320 across, about 165 tall where it was 400 (`PLANET`: curved to a radius of 1400, kept to 28 degrees). That it is a piece of a planet is learnt on the scroll, from the torn underside and the core.
- "I think we have too much forest surrounding it. I just wanted there to be like a nature area, not that it was completely covered and surrounded by nature. I think we should make the city bigger and utilize a lot of the space that's currently being tied up with the nature." **Nature is one area. The city takes the rest of the top of the world.**
- "The nature area should honestly look like a national forest, not something that is desert like, think of like a jungle. I wouldn't even hate like small mountains somewhere as well to give it more 3-D depth, even if that's outside the forest. Water sources would be interesting as well." **The nature area is lush, a forest as dense as a jungle, with small mountains and water.**
- "In Star Wars, the Star Trek, you often see flying vehicles." **Flying vehicles, plainly seen.**
- *Fable's calls in answering it, his to overturn:*
    - The nature area is at the left of the world, behind and round the pyramid, so the temple stands at a jungle's edge as one does in those films, with the small mountains behind it on the skyline.
    - **The jungle stays in the site's colors and is not green, by his own earlier rule ("Any nature is in the site's colors, not green").** If a jungle that is not green reads wrong to him, that rule is the one to change.
    - The spire is 280 and the tallest buildings 215, so the city is the larger thing at rest. On a tall screen the world rests lower, so its torn edge stays under the foot of the screen: this replaces "a taller screen shows more of the world" above a set depth.
    - The flying vehicles run in lanes between the towers, as traffic, with a few larger craft.
    - With the cap this shallow the world is about 915 from the spire's tip to the lowest of what broke away.

**The finished version, as planned by Fable for building (Nov 15). Items 19 to 27 above are the agreement. This is how each is being met, and every call here is his to overturn.**

- **Item 19, the city's reach:** the city ends at 472 on the map, where the torn edge is at about 561 to 664, so about three quarters of the way out. It is as dense as it was, about 110 buildings where there were 213, with a ragged fringe so it does not end on a line. Four wedges cut into it: the jungle at the left, the dunes at the right, and two of open ground at the front. Item 19's "58 of the dome's 78 degrees" was written for the first dome, which he has since had flattened twice: the build keeps its meaning, a quarter of the way in.
- **Item 20, bare ground at the rim:** from 472 to the torn edge nothing is built. The land's builder has it as bare ground, with the broken margin at its outer side.
- **Item 21, polished metal:** every builder on top of the world has one recipe (`reference/kits/skycity.md`, "Polished metal"): a mirror, never a glow or a pale flat color, shaped to catch light. The stage's reflected room was rebuilt to give it things to reflect: the moon, the glow, strip lights overhead, at each side and in front. Whole towers clad in bronze and gold, copper domes, gilded rings on the spire, a mirror cap and metal casing on the pyramid, veins of metal coming up through the bare ground, and pools of mirror polished metal: one in each open wedge, one by the pyramid, one among the dunes.
- **Item 22, the jungle:** trees 70 to 120 tall with giants to 170, as tall as the buildings beside them, crown to crown.
- **Item 23, the second natural area: dunes.** A sea of pale gold sand at the right of the world, on the moonlit side, with a pool of mirror polished metal among them. The opposite of the jungle in every way: pale where it is dark, low and smooth where it is tall and tangled.
- **Item 24, the monument: a colossal Sun disc** of polished gold standing on its rim, about 190 across, carved as Work's Sun stones are, with a boss and no face. It stands where the crag was, in the dunes at the right of the skyline: a circle at the right to match the pyramid's triangle at the left, with the spire between. The mountains at the left, behind the pyramid, are dropped with it, and the land is level.
- **Item 25, the craft:** five to eight designed craft, each 45 to 90 long, and a few thin lanes of small lights threading the city's canyons. The builder is told to recommend none if they cannot be made good.
- **Item 26, more kinds of building:** the city's builder is told ten buildings a stranger can call ten things. The gate at the end of the way to the spire is being made again in polished metal, or removed.
- **All water is dropped** with the mountains: the pools of metal are where the lake was.
- **The port is dropped** with its crag.
- **Item 27:** the torn underside, the core, the debris, the sky and the layout are not being worked on. Stills of them from before are kept to compare.

**The finished version, as built (Nov 15). It stands, at `/lab/hero` and on top of `/lab/whole`, and is on the live site. Every item of 19 to 27 is built. None of what is new has been called a film or a AAA frame by a fresh reviewer.**

- **Item 19 and 20:** the city ends at 472 on the map with a ragged fringe, about 80 buildings on 119 lots, and bare cracked bedrock runs from it to the torn edge.
- **Item 21, polished metal:** the spire's rings, clamps and needle in gold, its cage in copper, its piers in bronze. The pyramid cased in bands of gold with a faceted mirror cap. About four in ten of the city's towers clad in fluted bronze, copper or gold. A ring of polished bronze where the way ends, with two more seen through it. The Sun disc. Three pools of polished bronze on the land and one in the dunes. Every metal reflects a room made for it and shows the cursor's light as a soft panel that moves (`src/pieces/skycity/polish.ts`).
- **Item 22:** about 150 great trees, 70 to 120 tall with giants to 186, palms over the canopy, right up to the pyramid.
- **Item 23:** dunes of pale gold sand at the right, round the monument, with a pool of polished metal.
- **Item 24:** a Sun disc of gold, 168 across, on its rim in a granite cradle between obelisks, carved with the Sun stone's marks round a boss. No face.
- **Item 25:** five designed craft, 46 to 92 long. The lanes of small lights were tried, never read as traffic, and were left out.
- **Item 26:** the city rebuilt as blocks of joined buildings and towers in five designs. The two blank blocks are gone: the ring stands there.
- **Item 27:** the torn underside and the debris were put back exactly as they were in the build he saw (their builders had gone on after it), and the room's light was brought back to what it was, so stills of the underside, the core and the debris from before and after agree but for a slightly lighter rock.
- **What the builders' own fresh reviewers last said, plainly:** the city "a painted tabletop miniature", "a 1930s Art Deco downtown". The pyramid "a backlight behind louvres". The Sun disc "a well-modelled prop from a mid-tier game", "a collectible coin", and next to Work's stones "a cousin, not the ancestor". The jungle "a dense, rust-brown jungle" at a glance and "amateur" close up. The land's margin "a layered band" and its pools "lava decals". The craft read as craft in open sky and "a brass desk ornament" close up. The spire's gilding has not been judged by a fresh reviewer.
- **Measured:** WebKit at an iPad's size, no errors, on both pages. The test browser holds 60 frames a second at the median with the world's canvas held to 2.8 million pixels, with some frames at 30. Not measured on a 120 Hz screen. The world takes about 1.3 seconds to build in slices, the debris and the torn underside most of it, and its first frame up to 1.9 seconds on a cold cache.
- **All work on the hero's world as built is stopped (Nov 15, to Claude Code).** "Stop all work on the hero's world. I am redesigning it with the web chat from a clean slate (Study 32, /lab/hero-blockout). Leave the built world committed as it is and do not build on it."
    - In exact terms: `src/pieces/skycity/` stays in the repo as it was pushed and is not changed, not improved and not used as a base. Nothing in it was changed by the fix for the crash, which is all in shared code.
- **The built world is off the hero's screen for now (Nov 15, to Claude Code).** "We are replacing the hero's 3D objects, so no point in wasting tokens doing work on it. You can simply just take it off the screen for now." "I just meant the main 3D city planet objects. Everything up in the sky should remain as is."
    - In exact terms: on `/lab/hero` and at the top of `/lab/whole` the planet with its city, its torn underside, its core and its debris is not mounted. The sky is exactly as it was: stars, moon, band, meteors and the glow. His words, the buttons and the layout are where they were, and the world's place on the page is kept empty, so Work stands where it stood.
    - Fable's call, his to overturn: the six small streams that left the torn face go with the world, since they would hang from nothing. The main stream stays and still runs into Work, setting off from where the core's underside was.
    - One switch brings it all back: `WORLD.shown` in `src/hero/board.ts`. The new world goes in that place.

- **The rock round the core is to be as good as picture 9's. His ask (Nov 17, 23:11), with a screenshot of the live hero and picture 9 beside it, the broken rock round the core circled roughly in red ("don't take it too literal").** "I want to take a look at the planet around the core, the broken part. If you compare it to the Gemini image, you'll notice that theirs looks super more detailed than ours, and it's because of where their cracks are and how the lighting interacts with it. We should do the same workflow we did earlier, where we have critics review and make sure that we're making something that looks just as good as the image."
    - **In exact terms:** the torn rock of the hollow (`rock` in `src/pieces/world/`), from the roof under the land down to the floor under the ball and out to the skin each side. Today it is thin flakes, pale near the ball, with flat black masses beside them. Picture 9's is great blocks with clean broken faces, each with a face lit by the core and a side in shadow, divided by cracks that follow the rock's own joints. The bar is his: as good as the picture.
    - **How:** rounds as before. An Opus builder from a brief, two fresh Opus reviewers, Fable judging beside picture 9 and writing the next brief. He is shown it when it is at the bar or when Fable can say plainly where it falls short.
    - **Six rounds, and Fable has put the solid rock on the page for him to judge (Nov 18). Not ruled: his to overturn, and the rock before it is one switch away (`?rock=r`).** Scores from the two fresh reviewers of each round: 4 and 4.5, 5 and 5.5, 5 and 5, (the fourth was cut off), 5 and 4.5, 5.5 and 5. What it is now: one solid cave of broad broken sheets with the ball sunk in a socket, its hue at each brightness measured off picture 9, and picture 9's large pattern of light and dark laid over it as a soft map (blurred until none of the picture's own edges can be found in it), so its brights and darks fall where the picture's do. **Where it is short of the picture, plainly:** less vivid (2.1 percent of it is bright against the picture's 3.7, and too much of it is a mid brown), less crisp (0.28 of its lit pixels are on an edge against 0.39), the roof's front reads close up as a net of flat stones, and the map leaves a few soft dark smudges on lit faces. **Why it is on the page anyway:** beside the rock before it, which is thin flakes with black air between them, it has weight and one readable light, which is what he asked for. He called the exploded planet the coolest part of the site, so if he prefers the vivid one it goes back in a minute.

### Work

- One section. A centered title. No featured card.
- Categories at the top, Software first: Software, Games, 3D art, Photography. A project lives in one category only, and Brandon says which.
- Four or six items per category on the home page.
- On the home page: four, in two straight rows of two. The categories are a bar of their own below the title. (Nov 12)
- How the stream runs through the section is in The stream, "The stream through Work". (Nov 12)
- A View more button goes to an All work page with the same categories, showing everything. There are no separate pages for games, photography or 3D art.
- 3D art stays visible while we build and is hidden before launch if he has nothing to show.
- Photography is one category. He picks the photos. Boudoir is never named as its own category.

### Cards and buttons

- A card is a card: picture, title, description and tags. Brass is an accent on it, not the whole card.
- Keep the tags and the text. The second build lost them.
- The second build's spacing was far too cramped.
- He likes the second build's brass buttons, the Download PDF one most. He wants a primary and an accent, and both react to the light.
- On the first copper buttons (Nov 11): the words on the bright one are hard to read, because its face is light on the top half and dark on the bottom half, right behind the words. He wants a face that makes sense and does not put that split behind the text, and floated a tarnish look. He also does not love how simple the hover was (the button only lifted).
- On the second pass (Nov 11): "The buttons do look better." He likes "especially how the mouse moving around changes the glow on them". The other hover effects (the light running round the border, the lift, the accent's words warming) "aren't bad though I don't necessarily love them either". The hover is not settled.
- "Don't make any changes to the colors for now." He said it when asking whether the buttons are 508 compliant. (Nov 11)
- The hover is Catch: the cut border, the edges and the words light only where the cursor is near. "I think my favorite is catch." Coming in and moving around "feels about perfect". (Nov 11)
- Still to fix on Catch, in his words: on mouse leave "it takes a bit longer than I'd like for the animation to fade off", and "the button doesn't scale or do anything else. I think we should add some type of lift or scale. I'm leaning more towards a scale". (Nov 11)
- The cursor's light as it moves around the page "looks amazing" on the dark buttons and is "a lot harder to see" on the bright ones. "I don't want to nerf the secondary buttons." "I wish the primary buttons had a bit more shine to them." He floated darkening the bright button a little and asked whether that would look off. (Nov 11)
- Under the cursor the button grows a little. "I really like the scale." (Nov 11)
- The bright button is the shinier one: a little darker, under a clear coat that shows the cursor's light crossing it. "I like the primary with the most shine!" (Nov 11)
- The cards are designed with the words they will really carry. "We should build these out with the content they're actually going to have on them. For now we can just do a placeholder image." (Nov 12)
- His idea for a card's picture, wanted but not yet designed: "My portfolio's current site does something where the images are kind of like black-and-white before you hover them. It'd be cool if we could do something similar, but with like a bronze effect or something that matches our color scheme." In exact terms: at rest a card's picture is shown in one tone taken from the palette, and under the cursor it shows its true colors. (Nov 12)
- **The card as proposed is approved as the base (Nov 12).** "No, everything in the proposal is fine." What that covers:
    - On it, top to bottom: the picture in a window sunk into the card. A small label of the kind of project and the year. The title. The description. Up to four tags. The whole card opens the project.
    - Made of: the dark plate from the stream's test shot. Metal only as accents: a thin copper bead all round, which the stream lands on and strikes, and the frame round the picture. The buttons' metal and lighting.
    - The picture rests in bronze and shows its own colors under the cursor or with keyboard focus, when the bead and the frame catch the light and the card grows a little. Where nothing hovers, the cards nearest the middle of the screen show their colors. **Changed Nov 14: only on a phone.** On anything larger no card lights by itself ("No card lights by itself", below).
    - A card is always readable. The stream's strike lights it and never hides it.
    - The derby uses the four projects on his live home page with their real words, and his real screenshots in place of a blank. Which four end up on the home page is still his to say.
- **No card lights by itself on anything larger than a phone (Nov 14).** "On mobile devices and on iPad when scrolling, the cards automatically light up with color. I understand this is probably best user experience for mobile users, but most of my testing is being done on a iPad right now, which is causing issues. Let's go ahead and remove that feature. For everything outside of mobile devices, anything bigger than a mobile device should not automatically have a card come in focus. It should have to be clicked beforehand or hovered by a mouse or an Apple pencil."
    - In exact terms: on any screen larger than a phone, a card shows its own colors only when it is tapped, or hovered by a mouse or a pencil, or has keyboard focus. Scrolling lights nothing.
    - On a phone the cards nearest the middle of the screen still light as the page scrolls. Read from "I understand this is probably best user experience for mobile users". His to overturn. The phone is designed in the mobile pass.
    - Built Nov 14, in `WorkCard.tsx`. Calls made for him, none a rule:
        - A phone is a touch screen whose shorter side is under 600 px (`PHONE_SIDE`). An iPad is not one, held either way.
        - **A finger's first tap lights a card and does not open it. A tap on a lit card opens it.** A click cannot bring a card into focus if it also leaves the page, which is how "clicked beforehand" is read. It means two taps to open a case study on an iPad, where it was one.
        - A tap anywhere else lets the card go back to rest, and a tap on another card moves the light to it.
        - A tap on a sleeping card wakes it by its band, from the edge nearest the tap, and does not open it.
        - A finger that scrolls the page lights nothing, even when it starts on a card.
        - A mouse and a pencil are as on a desktop: hovering lights the card and one click opens it.
- What he asked for with the approval (Nov 12): "Maybe you could figure out if there are any data points that could be important or nice to have that we don't, and maybe it makes sense in the layout of the card itself. Maybe you could also give me a few different variants, but only ones that are top-tier. I don't just want variants for the sake of it."
- **His verdict on the card derby (Nov 13).** Nothing on the cards changes until he says: "Let's hold up on changing anything for the moment."
    - **The layout is C · Data plate.** "When it comes to data plate, I genuinely like that one a lot."
    - **Cast and Etched are out.** "Cast and etched look really bad and are pretty much unusable." They are in Dropped.
    - **Toned is the best of the three and not loved.** "I don't necessarily love toned either. It's just better than the other ones. I think the hard part here is making the cards look good with the pictures that we're using."
    - His question, not a ruling: "Is it customary to show a picture of the project? I know it makes sense on 3D projects and stuff, but a lot of my projects are simply, here's what a dashboard looks like on this app or something."
    - **Where a click goes has to be plain.** "I see you have Live and Code, which are really nice features to be able to do that from the home page, but the main arrow that moves on hover, which is supposed to take the user to the actual expanded project or case study page, whatever the appropriate name is for it, should have that context. Maybe we could put that in the bottom right corner and it just says case study, so when you hover the card that arrow moves and lets you know this is going to take you to the case study, and then if you hover Live or Code, it goes away, giving the user context so they understand that this takes me to the live site, not the case study." In exact terms: on C the arrow beside the title has no words. He wants the words "Case study" with the arrow in the card's bottom right corner. With the pointer anywhere on the card they light and the arrow moves. With the pointer on Live, Evals or Code they go quiet and that link lights, so it is always plain which place a click leads to. The name of the page is not settled. Not built.
    - **The hover is not there yet.** "I found the hover effect to be slightly underwhelming, and I don't know if I like that line of light that moves back and forth on the cards. It doesn't look bad but it doesn't really look great either." The line is the slanted streak the cursor's light makes on the picture's glass.
    - What he expects of the stream: "We also have to consider our stream. That's going to add a lot to the card, or at least I'm hoping it is: it will light up the card and the card will react to it in certain ways. So we already have that going for it."
- **The pictures get retaken, and he leans toward loops (Nov 13).** "No matter what I need to retake pictures, so these will not be the pictures that will be used in the final version. I'm open to pictures or loops. Maybe even both, though if we had to choose one I'm leaning more loops." He asked for Fable's recommendation: both, as one thing. A card's loop is a short recording of the product in use, and its picture is that recording's first frame. The picture is what rests in bronze, what shows where motion is turned off, and what is there before the loop has arrived. Not a ruling until he says so.
- **The second round, built on his word (Nov 13).** "Go ahead and do the next round of changes now." At `/lab/work-cards`:
    - The streak of light on the picture's glass is cut. The glass throws no glare back.
    - "Case study" with the arrow is in the card's bottom right corner, as he described: lit while the pointer is on the card, quiet while it is on Live, Evals or Code, when that link lights.
    - The card is one layout, the data plate, and its picture rests one way, toned. The layouts and treatments he passed over are out of the code. Their stills are in `reference/studies/23-*`.
    - A card can play a loop. Nothing plays yet: there are no recordings.
    - Two of the stand in pictures are cropped tight, to show what one telling part at a readable size does for a card.
- **What he wants from the third round (Nov 13). Talk first:** "Before you make any changes, let's think about how we would like to update this."
    - The foot of the card: "I like the look of data plate the best, but I actually really like the border that set in has at the bottom, and then having all the links at the bottom. So Live, Code on the left and then View project on the right." In exact terms: the data plate keeps its picture and its band of bronze. Under the tags it gets the thin line Set in had, and below that line one row: Live, Evals and Code on the left, the link to the project's own page on the right. The links leave the band. Whether that link reads "Case study", as he said earlier in the day, or "View project", as he said here, is not settled.
    - The line of light again: "I don't like the animated line of light that's going through the card faces over the image, especially when I'm not even moving my mouse around them." It was cut in the second round, which went up half an hour before he wrote this. What he describes, with Set in and "View project" on screen, is the first round. What moved it while his mouse was still is the lamp, which drifts on its own when the cursor is idle.
    - **More of an object.** "What do you think about more of a 3D effect when you hover it: it scales up, and you could drag your mouse along the sides of the card to turn the perspective of it in 3D? That's kind of more like what I was envisioning for this." In exact terms: under the pointer the card grows and turns in depth, following where the pointer is on it.
    - "I'm open to additional changes as well to give it more of a 3D look, whether that means increasing the border slightly, having it turn inward to make it look like it has more 3D depth, a bigger box shadow behind it that will appear when the light source hits it."
    - What comes after: "After this next iteration, we should probably hand it over to Claude Code and have him build it onto the site so we can fine tune from there."
- **The third round, built on his word (Nov 13).** He reloaded: "Yes, that light is gone." What he asked for:
    - "For the third round, we don't have to go overboard. We can keep it pretty simple. I just want to get an idea of how much degrees it will turn and how the light will look shimmering around the card."
    - **The way the colors came back is out.** "One thing I don't like though is the animation that makes the screenshots actual color. It's like not very good. I don't like that it comes from a circle. It seems like it lags a bit." The colors spreading from the pointer are in Dropped.
    - "What I'd like to see is a few variations of that and a few variations of the different tilts or 3D effects that you propose, and then once I pick the ones I want we can do a handoff to Claude Code and have him add this to the work page."
    - The frame rate: "The frame rate is a bit low already when hovering the cards. It's probably a code issue, because I don't think we have anything too crazy on this, and it doesn't really need to be fixed because we're about to code the final version in Claude Code anyways." Not fixed here. It goes in the handoff as something to measure and find the cause of.
    - Built at `/lab/work-cards`, one card with a switch for each choice and the grid below it. **Waiting on his picks:**
        - How the picture's colors come back: A · Fade. B · Fade, with the picture coming a little closer. C · Sweep, behind a soft slanted edge from the left. D · Lights first, the picture's darks last.
        - How far the card turns under the pointer: none, 3, 6 or 10 degrees at its edge.
        - Which way it leans: the side under the pointer goes away, or comes toward the pointer.
        - How much it grows: 1.5, 3 or 5 percent.
        - The edge: the thin bead, or a wide rim that turns in to a face that sits lower and throws a shadow onto it.
    - Built as he asked at 10:43: the thin line under the tags, with Live, Evals and Code on its left and the case study on its right. The band keeps the kind of project on its left and the year on its right.
    - Fable's call, his to overturn: the link reads "Case study". He said "case study" and then "view project". "View project" could as well mean the running site, which is the confusion he raised himself.
- **His picks from the third round (Nov 13).** "These are the settings I want to go with." From the switches at `/lab/work-cards`:
    - The picture's colors come back as A · Fade: the whole picture at once.
    - The card turns 3 degrees at its edge under the pointer.
    - It leans away: the side under the pointer goes back.
    - It grows 1.5 percent.
    - The edge is Deep: the wide rim that turns in to a face that sits lower, and throws a shadow onto it.
    - `CARD_LOOK` in `src/components/WorkCard.tsx` holds them, and the study page opens on them and keeps its switches. Still: `reference/studies/26-work-card-as-picked.jpg`.
    - What he passed over is in Dropped. The amounts can still be tuned once the card is on the Work page: "have him build it onto the site so we can fine tune from there".
    - What comes next, in his words: "Let's go ahead and write up the handoff document so this can be built into the current working work page." The agreement for that build is in The stream, "The second pass on the Work page".
- **One size of card, and the Software card is it (Nov 13).** "We need to make all cards the same size. Naturally we should probably use our software development cards as the standard. Every category has a different size. This does limit and set the standard for the amount of content that could be on a card. I currently think the software development cards are pretty much perfect, with one caveat. I think we should keep one row of tags. Currently the AI glasses one breaks that and has four tags that exceed one row, and because we only have one row all the cards should shrink to accommodate this. Currently the other three cards in software engineering have this little space that can accommodate a second row of tags. We should get rid of that and set that as a standard across all categories."
    - In exact terms: at any one screen width, every card in every category is the same size. The Software card sets it: its picture, its band, a title, its description, one row of tags and its foot. The room a second row of tags takes today comes out of every card.
    - It changes "Up to four tags" in the approved base to: as many tags as fit one row.
    - What follows from it: the words are written to fit the card. The card does not grow to fit the words.
    - Fable's calls, his to overturn:
        - A card shows the tags that fit one row, in the order they are written, and no more. On the AI Glasses card that hides the fourth where the card is narrow. Which three it keeps for good is his, since the words are his.
        - His answer (Nov 13): "Regarding the glasses card, I see no issues with it. It's four tags that are perfectly fitting in a single row. I don't care how many tags you can fit in a single row, as long as it's one row." So the call above stands as a rule: one row, as many tags as fit it, and the AI Glasses card keeps its four where four fit.
        - A title is one line. At any one width the description has the same number of lines on every card, the most a Software card needs there.
        - The stand in cards for the other categories become that size. With every card one size a row never changes height, so the stream settling onto a new height, and its test, come out.
        - Claude Code measures how many characters a title, a description and a row of tags can hold, and writes it here, so he has the numbers when he rewrites the words.
    - **How much a card holds, measured on `/lab/work` (Nov 13).** A card is 341 px wide on a page 1100 wide, the narrowest that shows two columns. From 1440 wide up it is 453 px and wider, and its words grow with it, so the counts stop changing there.
        - **A title: 32 characters** to fit one line at every desktop width. From 1280 wide up it holds 36.
        - **A description: about 225 characters.** That is 4 lines of about 61 from 1440 wide up. A narrower card gets more lines, not fewer words: 5 lines of about 52 at 1194 wide, 6 lines of about 48 at 1100.
        - **A row of tags: three tags of up to 13 characters each** to show at every desktop width, or four of up to 9, or two of up to 21. From 1440 wide up a row holds three of up to 17, or four of up to 12.
        - As the words stand today: every title and description fits. Below about 1400 wide the AI Glasses card shows three of its four tags, and below about 1240 wide the Design System Extractor card shows two of its three ("Live component capture" is 22 characters).
        - On a phone a card is about 243 px wide. There a title has two lines and a description 8, and the counts are his when the mobile pass comes.
- **What a card's loop and picture need. Fable's spec, his to change:** one recording a project, of the product doing the one thing that makes it worth a look. Twice as wide as it is tall, 1600 by 800 or larger. Four to eight seconds, ending close enough to where it starts to cut cleanly. MP4, H.264, no sound. The browser zoomed in before recording, so one part fills the frame and its type reads at the card's size. The picture is the first frame, saved as an image.
- **What was in front of him at first: the card derby at `/lab/work-cards` (Study 14).**
    - How the picture rests: 1 · Toned, 2 · Cast, 3 · Etched. Fable recommends Toned. **Cast leans against "Brass is an accent on it, not the whole card"**, because the picture is the largest thing on the card.
    - The layout: A · Set in (the picture inside the card with its own frame). B · Edge to edge (the picture fills the top of the card to the bead). C · Data plate (edge to edge, with a band of dark bronze under the picture that carries the kind, the year and the links, and no footer). Fable recommends C.
    - Facts Fable added to the card, his to overturn: the year. Links straight to the running project, its public evals and its code, where it has them. The words "View project" with an arrow on A and B, an arrow beside the title on C.
    - Facts Fable left off, with the reason: a line of one measured number (it needs new words from him, and the tags already carry it). His role (it is the same on every card). How long it took.
    - Fable's calls in the build, his to overturn: the picture's window is two to one, not sixteen to nine, because his screenshots are wide and a lower window keeps the section shorter. All four years are shown as 2026.

### Skills

- The workbench is the skills piece.
- One fixed light: the desk lamp and the lit monitor. Choosing a skill never relights the desk.
- Skills are never tied to objects on the desk.
- Skills read in wide rows, not narrow columns.
    - **On the home page the rows sit under the desk (Nov 14).** "I like the content under the desk better." Said after seeing both layouts with his real words in the fuller draft. It replaces the line written that morning from his approval of the rough draft, which had the rows in a column beside the desk. The rows beside the desk are in Dropped.
- One animation for skills. Nothing is hidden behind a category.
- **The desk and the section's content come onto the page when the stream reaches them (Nov 14).** "I also want the surge to drive the appearance of these sections: it hits a certain point and my profile picture fades in and the content appears, something similar with the desk as well."
    - In exact terms: Skills is not on the page until the stream's leading end reaches a point. Then the desk appears and the section's content with it. He wrote "the surge". Fable reads it as the stream's leading end as the visitor scrolls, not the surge a change of category sends through Work, and said so to him.
    - It sets "One fixed light" above aside for the arrival only, as Fable reads it: the desk is dark until the stream reaches it, and once lit its lights are fixed and choosing a skill never relights it.
    - Where the points are, and whether things go away again when the stream draws back, are not ruled. Fable's calls are in Open 28.
- **The desk has a real desk's proportions (Nov 14).** "On the skill section, the desk looks weird. It looks very elongated. I understand this is just a rough draft, but that's something we should probably address."
    - In exact terms: the long desk in the fuller draft, about 1160 units long on legs about 220 tall, reads as stretched. The desk is to look like a real desk. How long it is, is not ruled.
- **The skills on the live site are out of date, and his résumé is the newer source (Nov 14).** "Are we limiting the number of skills we have to fit the layout? It just seems a bit lighter than what's currently on my portfolio, which, to be honest, my current portfolio is way out of date. I have much more skills now that I'm capitalizing on versus back when I originally built the portfolio. I can share my résumé with you." He shared it.
    - Nothing was cut for the layout: the live site lists 48 skills and the fuller draft carried all 48. It read lighter because the live site gives each skill a chip with an icon and the draft set them as running lines. His résumé lists 77, in six groups.
    - It changes Open 6 and Words, "His live site's content is the base", for the lists of skills: the résumé's lists are the base. Which skills the site ends up showing is his to say.
- **The desk is A · Bench (Nov 14).** "My favorite desk is option A." "I like what you did with the desk!" Picked from the three rough desks at `/lab/desk`: the joiner's bench, a thick slab on two trestles with a bronze angle over the front edge and two drawers under it. B and C are in Dropped. Fable had recommended B with C's rail.
- **What he wants of the scene on the desk (Nov 14).** "I feel like we could definitely improve on the scene quite a bit in multiple different areas." His notes, each in his words:
    - **The PC is to look higher end, like his PC Builder's.** "I wish the computer looked a bit more higher end though. Let me send you a screenshot of one from my PC Builder." The screenshot is of a build in a glass case with two chambers: three fans over the top, three in a side wall, three in the floor, one at the back, a liquid cooler with a lit ring, four sticks of memory, a graphics card with a lit bar, sleeved cables, a strip of light round the base.
    - **A large desk mat.** "The desk would benefit greatly from like a large mouse pad on it, maybe centered where the user's iPad, Apple pen, keyboard, mouse and camera are? Taking up like 80% of the desk, maybe stopping right before the monitor."
    - **An ultrawide monitor.** "Speaking of the monitor, it looks a bit small on this desk. We could probably go with an ultrawide monitor instead. It would fit really well."
    - **The left of the desk looks vacant.** "I'm hoping those fill the gap on the left-hand side of the desk. It just looks a little vacant."
    - **A wireless keyboard, and a gaming one.** "Another issue I have with the desk is the wired keyboard. Let's get rid of the wire. I use wireless. I think it looks bad." "I tend to enjoy higher end products. Think like a gaming keyboard with RGB lights going on it. Of course we could keep the RGB within our color scheme, but I think we could do something fun with it."
    - **A high end gaming mouse.** "The mouse itself looks like a very old mouse." "For the mouse, same thing. I like high end gaming mice."
    - **A better camera.** "The camera leaves a lot to be desired as well."
    - In exact terms: the things on the desk are his own kind of gear, high end and current, and each is made to the PC Builder's standard. It changes "Nothing new" in the second of the five calls below: the mat is new.
    - Lights stay in the site's colors, as he said of the keyboard and as the fourth call has it for the PC. His screenshot's reds, greens and blues are the PC Builder's own, not this page's.
- **The desk as drawn in the second round is right (Nov 14).** "Yeah, everything looks good here." Its proportions stand: about 2 m long, 0.78 deep and 0.74 tall, seen from the front left and above, about 860 units wide on the page.
- **The lists of skills are the résumé's, as the second round shows them (Nov 14).** The same approval. He did not ask for any of the skills that are on the live site and not on the résumé. They stay off until he says otherwise, and the words are still his to rewrite.
- **The desk as a 3D piece: the five calls, agreed with one addition (Nov 14).** "The only thing that looked potentially off was on the desk you didn't mention the Apple pencil next to the iPad Pro. Besides that, everything looks good that you mentioned."
    1. It is made in code in this repo, as his PC Builder was.
    2. On it: the monitor, the lamp, the keyboard and mouse, his iPad Pro with the Apple Pencil next to it, and his camera. Nothing new.
    3. The desk itself: a dark timber top, a bronze front edge that carries the stream, two drawers with the knob he chose for the sleeping card.
    4. Under it: his PC Builder's case, lit in the site's ember.
    5. The headphones are their own small piece by the title, of the site's own design.
    - In exact terms, his addition: Fable's list said "a tablet". The tablet is his iPad Pro, and its pencil lies beside it.
    - Fable's call, his to overturn: the tablet and the pencil are modeled to read as his, and carry no maker's mark, as with the headphones.
    - It settles Open 5 for this piece, and "One fixed light" above gives way as Open 28 said it would: the PC is a third light, and the desk's own lights are fixed once it is lit.
- **The rows of skills are cut down (Nov 14).** "For the skill section, we have three columns. I think we can remove the second column. It doesn't really add anything, and the first column could use some work too. There's like a catchphrase that's super generic underneath each thing that can totally be removed. It doesn't add anything. I also think the category of the row should be centered with the skills. For full stack engineering, we should probably add a gaming subcategory where we go over the gaming skills that I have, and we should review the skills that we have. We don't have to keyword stuff as much as we do on our résumé and really can only list the big important ones."
    - In exact terms, five changes to each row:
        1. The middle column, the sentence of proof, goes.
        2. The line under each skill set's name goes.
        3. The skill set's name is centered top to bottom against its lists.
        4. Full-Stack Engineering gets a group for his game development skills.
        5. The lists are cut to the big, important skills. The résumé's lists are written to be matched by keyword and the site's need not be.
    - It replaces "The lists of skills are the résumé's, as the second round shows them" below. Which skills stay is his to say. Fable's first cut is in Open 30.
    - He kept the four skill sets. He did not take up Fable's idea of rows that follow a product's path, so the four stand.
- **Three skill sets. Product Leadership is cut (Nov 14).** "I wonder if we should just cut product leadership altogether. I think that's better said on the résumé and on the accomplishment section." He then spoke of "our 3 skills categories", so it is read as decided: AI Product Engineering, Full-Stack Engineering, Design Systems & Craft. Its skills leave the site's Skills section and are said in the résumé and in the accomplishments section when that comes back (Open 26).
- **No numbers on the rows, and more room between a row's groups (Nov 14).** "I think we can get rid of the numbers 1, 2, and 3 on our 3 skills categories. Next, the subcategory skill sections are a bit too close together. Maybe a bit more spacing would be better."
- **A small 3D thing in each row: his question, not a ruling (Nov 14).** "That leaves some space in the skill category sections. AI product engineering, full stack engineering, design systems and craft. I wonder if it makes sense to put a small 3D interactable object there. What do you think? It would fill the void and look pretty nicely, I think." Fable's answer is in Open 28.
- **The stream onto the desk's lamp is fine for now (Nov 14).** "I don't mind the stream on the desk lamp. I think that's fine. It may be updated later, but I'll need to see it in action to know." It is built that way, and he judges it in motion. With it, the third call's "a bronze front edge that carries the stream" becomes "a bronze front edge".
- **A derby for the small 3D things in the rows (Nov 14).** "With the 3D objects, I'd love to do a derby with them." It sets "One animation for skills" above aside for them, as Fable said in bold it would. Which three, and whether they stay, he says after the derby.
- **The stream may turn in to strike each row. His idea, to be seen (Nov 14).** "I also don't hate the idea of the stream turning slightly into the three rows as the stream comes down the page. This could have a striking effect, which makes that row visible. This would give us more strikes, more of a 3D feeling." Not a ruling. It is drawn in the fuller draft's seventh round, Open 28.
- **The stream strikes each row, and the headphones rock (Nov 14).** Asked whether the strikes at the rows and the rocking stay: "yes".
    - **What is thrown at a row's strike lands on the row's borders.** "The strike on the skill sections' 3D objects should allow the particles to fall on the borders within each skill section, so we need to treat the borders as if they're 3D objects." In exact terms: the rules over and under a row are solid things with an edge, as a card's edge is in Work. Shards thrown by a row's strike land on them, rest and fade.
    - **Nothing of a row shows before its strike, its 3D thing included.** "I kind of feel like the 3D objects should just be invisible, completely hidden, and there's just a strike that randomly happens that shows the row, and then the particles land in there and fade out. As you scroll, this happens 3 times." "I'm worried it might look weird to have 3 random 3D objects visible at the bottom of the screen." In exact terms, as Fable reads it and said to him: the strike lands on empty page where the row's thing will be, and the thing, the name and the lists come on at it. The draft already holds a row back this way. The still he saw had everything switched on.
    - **Parked, his idea, for Claude Code if the turn looks soft:** a border down the left of the rows, the same as the others and upright, so the stream bounces between it and the rows in a zigzag and does not just turn. "Not sure how good that would look though. I'm kind of 50-50 on that one. I don't think we have to build that right now. Maybe we can just build it as is, and if it looks soft that's something we could pass into Claude Code later to implement."
- **Each row's 3D thing comes to life when it is struck (Nov 14).** "We'll need some type of animation for the 3D object on the skill sections to come into life when they are struck. I don't know if it's like something that forms out of the surge or what. We can leave that up for Fable to figure out what he thinks would be appropriate. Just want to note that." What the animation is, is Fable's to design. Its proposal is in Open 28.
- **What he wants the three things to be: real things in fine detail, not simple forms (Nov 14).**
    - AI Product Engineering: "all of them seem a bit abstract, which is OK, but I think I want like some super hyper realistic CPU that kind of spins and has like a background and some other aspects to it around it or something."
    - Full-Stack Engineering: "I didn't really like any of the options you gave me. They seemed too simplified."
    - Design Systems & Craft: "I didn't really love the ones that you gave me. I'm thinking something more sophisticated. Think of, like, a painter's paint swatch, and then a paintbrush that kind of like swashes in between them or something. I'm not sure, but that's just something I'm thinking of."
    - In exact terms: each thing is a small scene made to the standard of the things on the desk, a real object with what belongs round it, not a bronze emblem. The CPU and the palette with a brush are his ideas and not yet picks. The first nine options are passed over.
- **His picks for the three things (Nov 14).** "I like option B better for AI product engineering. Option A for full stack and option A for design." From the derby at `/lab/skill-things`:
    - AI Product Engineering: the CPU in its socket, on a square of board with lit traces. Fable had recommended the CPU opened up.
    - Full-Stack Engineering: a screen with its back off.
    - Design Systems & Craft: the palette and brush.
    - The other three are in Dropped.
    - **How they come to life is not ruled.** "The only thing I'm on the fence about is the actual particle stream creating the objects. I'm worried they won't look good. I was hoping in the derby you could show me what that looks like, but the derby was too low quality." In exact terms: he has not agreed to the things being put together out of the strike's shards. The derby could not show it well enough to judge. It is proven at the bar, with the real stream, before it is settled. Open 28.
